# Research Target

Let \(p\) be prime and let \(d\) be an integer satisfying \(100<d<p\). The dimension is fixed at \(m=2\). Degrees \(d\le 100\), including the degenerate case \(d=0\), are outside the campaign scope and must not be treated as obstructions to the target. A point table is a function

\[
f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p.
\]

For every affine line \(L\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2\), a line table supplies a univariate polynomial \(P_L\) of degree at most \(d\), interpreted on \(L\). Define the test agreement

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],
\]

where \(L\) is uniform among affine lines and \(x\) is uniform on \(L\).

## Current benchmark

Kominers--Thaler--Zheng prove that there are absolute constants \(C,c>0\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}
\]

implies the existence of a total-degree-at-most-\(d\) polynomial \(Q\) satisfying

\[
\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

## Target theorem

The campaign seeks progress toward the following deliberately explicit research target.

For every fixed \(\eta>0\), prove constants \(C_\eta,c_\eta>0\) and a fully stated admissible parameter regime with \(100<d<p\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}
\]

implies

\[
\max_{\deg Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]
\ge c_\eta\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

Equivalent or stronger list-decoding conclusions are admissible only when the conversion to a single polynomial and its loss are proved.

## Milestones

- **Bivariate exponent improvement:** replace \(1/3\) by an explicit \(\alpha>1/3\) over prime fields.
- **Combinatorial improvement:** strengthen the incidence/concentration stage with its full parameter range.
- **Algebraic improvement:** strengthen the interpolation/factorization stage with complete prime-characteristic handling.
- **Near-linear exponent:** prove \((d/p)^{1-\eta}\) for every fixed \(\eta>0\).
- **Sharp threshold:** identify matching examples or prove a theorem at \(\Theta(d/p)\), if true.

Because \(d/p<1\), a larger exponent is a stronger result: it permits smaller local agreement.

The general-dimensional lift is standard once the bivariate statement is known. It is a downstream corollary, not a research milestone, and the campaign spends no agents on it.

## Lemma writing rule

**A lemma statement contains only its quantified objects, hypotheses, and conclusion. It contains no motivation, derivation, commentary, proof sketch, interpretation, history, or explanation; all such material belongs in the proof.**

The dedicated Lemma Writer post-edits every structured proof step without changing its mathematical content or status. It may split a source lemma into ordered pieces when that makes the logical structure easier to understand. Raw submissions remain immutable, and every edited statement is source-linked and checked deterministically with KaTeX before publication in the Lemma Book.

## Proof roadmap policy

Three synchronized roadmap agents maintain complete dependency DAGs for the algebraic engine, incidence engine, and end-to-end soundness chain. They share all submissions, audits, Lemma Book entries, peer roadmaps, and informal messages; each gives a majority of its attention to its assigned route. Roadmap progress is harness-derived. A node is verified only when it cites an exact source job, proof-step id, and response hash whose claim was independently accepted, and all of its dependencies are verified or external inputs. Agent work states and message-board claims never confer verification.

## Non-results

The following do not count as achieving an exponent milestone:

- a theorem only for fixed \(d\), unless clearly labeled;
- a hidden assumption \(p\ge d^C\) that makes the stated threshold vacuous;
- a list of many global polynomials without a proved single-polynomial conclusion;
- an exponent obtained after suppressing characteristic- or logarithmic losses;
- numerical success on small fields;
- a proof for line-versus-line, plane-versus-point, or axis-parallel tests presented as a line-versus-point theorem without a proved reduction.
- a result whose substantive advance concerns \(m\ne2\), extension fields, or dimension bootstrapping rather than the bivariate prime-field theorem.

ROADMAP algebraic-engine ROUND 3
SUMMARY: Round 3 keeps the bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy as the principal open input and replaces the previous whole-relation cleanup-plus-peeling branch by a mass-only factorwise route. Rank deficiency yields a squarefree relation; reached roots extend it to all carrier lines; primitive factor allocation leaves at most $K_\theta$ factors; characteristic-safe resultants delete at most $(D^2/d-D)/(p(p+1))+(D-d)/p$ normalized mass. One factor then retains $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ mass, and point-degree averaging supplies a simple seed with more directions than its weighted degree. Formal implicit uniqueness and line-factor divisibility force that factor to be $Z-Q$, so all disconnected pieces aggregate to one global degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial. New researcher-0009 and researcher-0010 inputs strengthen the graph-only and asymmetric square-root diagnostics but do not establish the bounded-weight handoff. The endpoint remains blocked on AE-12 and on standalone proof records for the factorwise assembly and simple-pencil lift.
NODE AE-01 [definition/candidate] deps=
Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, and $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$. For every affine line $L$, fix a bijective affine parametrization $\ell_L:\mathbb F_p\to L$ and $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\deg P_L\le d$. Put $$\mathcal I=\{(\ell_L(t),L):P_L(t)=f(\ell_L(t))\},\qquad \mu(E)=|E|/[p^2(p+1)],\qquad \epsilon=\mu(\mathcal I),$$ and let $\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)$ be the maximum of $p^{-2}|\{x:Q(x)=f(x)\}|$ over $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$.
NOTES: This fixes the bivariate prime-field regime and excludes both dimension bootstrapping and $d\le100$.
NODE AE-02 [definition/candidate] deps=AE-01
Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$. For an integer $D\ge d$, put $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}A\le D\},\qquad \rho_L(A)(t)=A(\ell_L(t),P_L(t)).$$ For a line family $R$, define $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\prod_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto(\rho_M(A))_{M\in R}.$$
NOTES: This is the Lean-level linear map measuring labeled graph constraints.
NODE AE-03 [definition/candidate] deps=AE-02
A rank-reaching certificate is $(G,R,D)$ where $G=(X,T,E)$ is a nonempty subgraph of $\mathcal I$, $R\subseteq T$, $D\in\mathbb Z$, $d\le D$, $|R|>D$, and $$\operatorname{rank}(\Phi_{R,D})<\dim V_D.$$ Every $L\in T$ must have more than $D$ distinct points $x$ such that $(x,L)\in E$ and $(x,M)\in E$ for some $M\in R\setminus\{L\}$.
NOTES: Only retained mass and reached roots are required; no minimum-degree hypothesis is built into the interface.
NODE AE-12 [obligation/open] deps=AE-03
For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, there exist constants $C_\theta>0$, $0<a_\theta\le1$, and $K_\theta\ge1$ such that, whenever $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta(d/p)^{1-\theta},$$ either $\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge a_\theta\epsilon$, or there is a rank-reaching certificate $(G,R,D)$ satisfying $$\mu(E)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,\qquad d\le D\le K_\theta d.$$
NOTES: This remains the principal campaign obligation. The direct branch is necessary because graph-only reaching families can be expensive even on immediately decodable instances.
NODE AE-13 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-12
After increasing $C_\theta$ as a function of $a_\theta,K_\theta,\theta$, every relation alternative satisfies $$D/(\epsilon p)\le a_\theta/[100(K_\theta+1)],\qquad D<p.$$
NOTES: The ratio is at most $K_\theta(d/p)^\theta/C_\theta$; increasing $C_\theta$ supplies all later loss absorption.
NODE AE-14 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-13
In the relation alternative, there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$\rho_M(A)=0\quad(M\in R),\qquad A_Z\ne0.$$ Take the product of the distinct irreducible factors of a nonzero kernel element. Each selected restriction remains zero because $\mathbb F_p[t]$ is a domain. If the result were $Z$-independent, more than $D$ distinct line equations would divide a nonzero polynomial of degree at most $D$. Finally, $D<p$ makes $Z$-dependence characteristic-safe.
NOTES: The cited interpolation steps supply nearby radicalization templates; the rank-general statement still needs a standalone implementation.
NODE AE-15 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-14
For every $L\in T$, $$\rho_L(A)=A(\ell_L(t),P_L(t))=0.$$ Indeed, each reached accepted point also lies on a selected line where $A$ vanishes, and both incident labels equal $f$ there. More than $D$ such roots force the identity.
NOTES: Distinct reached points give distinct roots of a restriction of degree at most $D$.
NODE AE-16 [reduction/drafting] deps=AE-15
Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the gcd of the $Z$-coefficients, $h=\deg H$, and $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}B$. Then $B$ is primitive, squarefree, and $Z$-dependent, with $h+\delta\le D$. Delete the at most $h$ lines contained in $H=0$ and factor $$B=c\prod_{i=1}^s C_i.$$ Every $C_i$ depends on $Z$, and $$s\le\deg_ZB\le\delta/d\le D/d\le K_\theta.$$ The remaining lines admit a disjoint allocation $T=\bigsqcup_iT_i$ such that $$C_i(\ell_L(t),P_L(t))=0\quad(L\in T_i).$$
NOTES: The 0007 and 0010 sources have enclosing verdict `revise`; they are used only as exact planning evidence.
NODE AE-09 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-16
Every allocated factor satisfies $$C_{i,Z}\ne0,
\qquad \gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1,$$ because $C_i$ is irreducible, $Z$-dependent, and $\operatorname{wdeg}C_i\le D<p<dp$. More generally, if coprime $U,V$ have weighted degrees $u,v$ and $Z$-degrees $r,s$, respectively, then the number of graph lines on which both restrictions vanish identically is at most $$su+rv-drs.$$
NOTES: Small weighted degree is a sufficient separability criterion. For reducible $B$, the condition $B_Z\ne0$ alone does not imply $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.
NODE AE-10 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-09
Put $$\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}C_i,
\qquad r_i=\deg_ZC_i,
\qquad \gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),$$ and $$\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).$$ If $b_i$ is the number of allocated lines on which $C_{i,Z}(\ell_L(t),P_L(t))$ vanishes identically, then $$b_i\le\min\{|T_i|,\Xi_i\},
\qquad \gamma_i\le\delta_i-d,
\qquad \Xi_i\le\delta_i^2/d-\delta_i.$$ Every other allocated line contains at most $\gamma_i$ accepted points where $C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))=0$.
NOTES: The factorwise source has an exact line audit but enclosing verdict `revise`; this node records the reusable bound without elevating its status.
NODE AE-17 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-10
Delete the content lines from AE-16, every identically ramified allocated line from AE-10, and every remaining accepted edge with $C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))=0$. If $E_{\mathrm{cl}}$ is the surviving edge set, then the deleted normalized mass satisfies $$\beta\le (D^2/d-D)/(p(p+1))+(D-d)/p\le a_\theta\epsilon/100.$$ Hence $$\mu(E_{\mathrm{cl}})\ge a_\theta\epsilon/2,$$ and every surviving edge assigned to $i$ satisfies $$C_i(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
NOTES: This replaces whole-relation cleanup and global peeling by an assigned-factor deletion charged directly to total mass.
NODE AE-18 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-17
Partition $E_{\mathrm{cl}}=\bigsqcup_iE_i$ by the assigned line factor and put $\nu_i=\mu(E_i)$. Since $s\le K_\theta$, some $i$ satisfies $$\nu_i\ge a_\theta\epsilon/(2K_\theta).$$ Moreover, AE-13 gives $$\nu_i(p+1)>D\ge\delta_i.$$ Averaging the $E_i$-degrees over all $p^2$ points therefore yields a point $b$ incident to more than $\delta_i$ distinct lines of $T_i$, with $$C_i(b,f(b))=0,
\qquad C_{i,Z}(b,f(b))\ne0.$$
NOTES: Only total edge mass is used; connectivity and inherited vertex degrees are unnecessary.
NODE AE-19 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-18
Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}C\le D<p$. Suppose $C(b,\alpha)=0$, $C_Z(b,\alpha)\ne0$, and more than $\Delta$ distinct lines $L_v(t)=b+tv$ carry polynomials $P_v$ of degree at most $d$ satisfying $$P_v(0)=\alpha,
\qquad C(b+tv,P_v(t))=0\text{ in }\mathbb F_p[t].$$ Then there are $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ and $u\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ such that $$C=u(Z-Q).$$ Applied at the seed of AE-18, this linearizes $C_i$.
NOTES: No accept-verdict source states this exact standalone package. The proof uses formal implicit uniqueness and divides only by the nonzero scalar $C_Z(b,\alpha)$; no factorials occur.
NODE AE-20 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-19
Let $C_i=u(Z-Q)$ be supplied by AE-19, and let $X_i$ be the point endpoints of $E_i$. Then $Q(x)=f(x)$ for every $x\in X_i$. Since $|E_i|\le(p+1)|X_i|$, $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge |X_i|/p^2\ge\mu(E_i)=\nu_i\ge a_\theta\epsilon/(2K_\theta).$$
NOTES: The irreducible factor is global, so all its disconnected clean pieces charge to the same polynomial without incidence mixing.
NODE AE-21 [theorem/blocked] deps=AE-20
For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, assuming AE-12, there are constants $C_\theta,c_\theta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta(d/p)^{1-\theta}$$ implies $\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\theta\epsilon$. One may take $$c_\theta=a_\theta/(2K_\theta).$$
NOTES: The direct alternative of AE-12 already has the conclusion; AE-13 through AE-20 close its relation alternative.
NODE AE-22 [theorem/blocked] deps=AE-21
For every fixed $0<\eta<1$, there exist constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every accepted-incidence instance of density $\epsilon$, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}$$ implies that one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES: Take $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. The endpoint remains blocked on AE-12 and on standalone proof records for the factorwise assembly and simple-pencil lift.
NODE AE-04 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-01
Every accepted graph of mass $\epsilon$ contains a subgraph of mass at least $\epsilon/4$ and minimum degree at least $\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$.
NOTES: This remains an off-path structural interface; its enclosing audit verdict is `revise`.
NODE AE-05 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-02
Let $G$ be a connected accepted subgraph of minimum degree $k$, let $\tau=|T|/[p(p+1)]$, and suppose $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,
\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2.$$ Then some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge k/(800p).$$
NOTES: This is the retained component-level algebraic baseline.
NODE AE-06 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-05
The AE-05 construction has $$D=\Theta(\sqrt{d\tau p^2/k}),
\qquad D<k,
\qquad \tau\ge k/(4p).$$ Consequently $k^2=\Omega(dp)$, so with $k=\Theta(\epsilon p)$ this route cannot reach below $\epsilon=\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$.
NOTES: This is an architectural ceiling for the accepted component construction, not for rank-aware interpolation.
NODE AE-07 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-01
For suitable legal constant-label instances with $1/(2K)\le\epsilon\le2/K$, every line family reaching half the accepted points of every accepted line has size at least $$p/(32\epsilon),$$ although a constant polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/2$.
NOTES: A uniformly small graph-only reaching family cannot replace AE-12's decoder alternative.
NODE AE-08 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-02
For positive integers $s,D,n,h$ with $d\le D<p$ and $n>D$, put $$C_s(D,d)=\sum_{a+b<s}\max(D-a-db+1,0).$$ If raw counting gives $$\dim V_D>nC_s(D,d),
\qquad sh>D,$$ then $$h^2>dn/12.$$
NOTES: The source audit is inexact with verdict `revise`; the statement is retained only as a diagnostic for raw uniform multiplicity counting.
NODE AE-11 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-01
For legal choices with $d+1<p$, there is a primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible polynomial $$C=XZ+H(Y),
\qquad \operatorname{wdeg}C=d+1,$$ supporting more than $d+1$ compatible degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines through one common lift, while no total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$ satisfies $C(X,Y,Q)=0$. At the common lift, $C_Z=0$.
NOTES: This shows that the simple-root hypothesis in AE-19 cannot be discarded.
NODE AE-23 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-01
Every raw accepted incidence graph is $K_{2,2}$-free. Moreover, for every prime $p$ and $101\le d<p$, there is a legal instance of density $\epsilon_0=d/p$ whose accepted carrier is connected and has full support, attains every point- and line-star lower envelope, and satisfies for $0<\vartheta\le1$ $$\vartheta p(p+1)/(d+1)\le\operatorname{OPT}_\vartheta\le\vartheta p(p+1)/(d-1),$$ while the polynomial $Q=0$ agrees with $f$ everywhere.
NOTES: The enclosing audit verdict is `revise`; this is off-path evidence about what AE-12 cannot be reduced to.
NODE AE-24 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-02
Let a nonempty accepted graph have point minimum degree $u$, line minimum degree $v$, $w=\min\{u,v\}$, and line measure $\tau$. If $$uv\ge2^{60}dp,
\qquad uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,$$ then some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge v/(800p).$$
NOTES: The enclosing audit verdict is `revise`. This strengthens the component baseline but does not provide the bounded-weight alternative in AE-12.
MSG reply -> incidence-engine refs=algebraic-engine:AE-12,algebraic-engine:AE-16,algebraic-engine:AE-17,algebraic-engine:AE-18,incidence-engine:IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY
The round-3 relation branch now needs only total accepted mass. From `AE-12`, `AE-16` allocates lines to at most $K_\theta$ factors, `AE-17` pays the factorwise ramification budget, and `AE-18` obtains a simple high-direction seed by averaging point degrees. No inherited minimum degree or degree-$D$ peeling is required; please retain the mass-only certificate.
MSG reply -> end-to-end-soundness refs=algebraic-engine:AE-11,algebraic-engine:AE-19,algebraic-engine:AE-20,researcher-0006:P7.1,researcher-0006:P9.1,researcher-0010:[P7].1
`AE-19` gives the requested standalone simple-seed lift with $\Delta\le D<p$. Formal implicit uniqueness identifies the degree-$d$ truncation with every supplied line label; then $C(X,Y,Q)$ has degree at most $\Delta$ and more than $\Delta$ distinct line factors, so irreducibility gives $C\sim Z-Q$. The simple seed remains essential by `AE-11`.
MSG objection -> global refs=algebraic-engine:AE-13,algebraic-engine:AE-16,algebraic-engine:AE-17,algebraic-engine:AE-18
The bounded-weight hypothesis performs two distinct jobs. It makes the uniform factorwise loss $$(D^2/d-D)/(p(p+1))+(D-d)/p$$ negligible relative to $\epsilon$, and it bounds the number of $Z$-dependent factors by $D/d\le K_\theta$. An inverse power of $\epsilon$ in $D/d$ breaks both `AE-17` and `AE-18`.
MSG objection -> incidence-engine refs=algebraic-engine:AE-12,algebraic-engine:AE-23,algebraic-engine:AE-24,researcher-0009:P12.1,researcher-0010:[P7].1
New corpus evidence reinforces that `AE-12` must be label-aware. `AE-23` records a connected full-support carrier attaining all star-moment lower envelopes and requiring fractional reaching weight $\Theta(p/\epsilon)$ even though $Q=0$ decodes globally. `AE-24` supplies a stronger asymmetric decoder, but its interpolation scale still has the square-root gate and does not yield $D=O_\theta(d)$.
ROADMAP incidence-engine ROUND 3
SUMMARY: Round 3 adds the asymmetric popularity-tail chain: if $u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d$, a two-sided high-tail core, a $\Theta(|T|/u)$ reaching sample, and the asymmetric decoder yield agreement at least $\epsilon/12800$. The central incidence obligation is therefore isolated to the complementary low-tail regime and then repackaged as the unconditional bounded-weight interface required by peer roadmaps. Exact point- and line-star envelopes, the connected regular carrier, integral and fractional reaching obstructions, bounded-order modal examples, and concurrency-corrected auxiliary DRC explain why label-blind sampling or fixed-order energy cannot prove that interface. The structured branch still asks for accepted mass $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ and $D=O_\theta(d)$; this makes vertical cleanup and degree-$D$ peeling near-lossless. The downstream route is shortened using the peer `E2E-21`--`E2E-23` idea: lift the common cleaned relation on each component, observe that each component polynomial contributes a divisor $Z-Q$ of the same $B$, bound the number of distinct component templates by $\deg_ZB\le D/d$, and pigeonhole edges. This replaces the former factor-partition and seed-factor-linearization path. The residual bounded-weight dichotomy remains open, and downstream drafting nodes remain subject to exact source and verifier checks.
NODE IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH [definition/candidate] deps=
Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and, for every affine line $L$, a bijective affine parametrization $L:\mathbb F_p\to L$ and $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\deg P_L\le d$. Define $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(L(t),L):P_L(t)=f(L(t))\},\qquad \rho=d/p,\qquad \epsilon=|E_{\rm acc}|/[p^2(p+1)],$$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d}\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For a nonempty $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$ put $$e(G)=|E|/[p^2(p+1)],\quad \mu(G)=|X|/p^2,\quad \tau(G)=|T|/[p(p+1)],$$ $$u(G)=\min_{x\in X}\deg_G(x),\qquad v(G)=\min_{L\in T}\deg_G(L).$$
NOTES: All normalization is for uniform affine incidences in the fixed bivariate prime-field scope.
NODE IE-D-RANK-REACH [definition/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
For an integer $D\ge d$, let $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\}$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ define $$\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E,\ \exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ (x,M)\in E\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $$\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<\dim V_D,$$ and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
NOTES: The definition allows $D\ge d$; the characteristic bound $D<p$ is derived only after the dichotomy.
NODE IE-D-POPULARITY-TAIL [definition/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
Assume $\epsilon>0$. For $$a_x=|\{L:(x,L)\in E_{\rm acc}\}|,
\qquad \delta_j=[p^2(p+1)]^{-1}\sum_{x:a_x\ge j}a_x,$$ define $$u_*=\max\{j\in\{1,\ldots,p+1\}:\delta_j\ge\epsilon/2\}.$$
NOTES: Universally, $u_*\ge\lceil\epsilon(p+1)/2\rceil$; this lower bound alone recovers only the cubic scale.
NODE IE-L-TAIL-CORE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-POPULARITY-TAIL
If $$u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d,$$ then the incidences supported on $\{x:a_x\ge u_*\}$ contain a nonempty accepted graph $G_*$ with $u=u(G_*)$, $v=v(G_*)$, $\tau=\tau(G_*)$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$ satisfying $$u\ge u_*/3,\qquad v\ge\epsilon p/16,$$ $$uv\ge2^{60}dp,
\qquad uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2.$$
NOTES: The indexed source step is conditional and its enclosing audit verdict is `revise`; the harness determines status.
NODE IE-L-ASYMMETRIC-SAMPLE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-TAIL-CORE
Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be nonempty, let $u=u(G)$, $v=v(G)$, $t=|T|$, $\tau=\tau(G)$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$. If $$uv\ge2^{60}dp,
\qquad uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\tau\ge u/[4(p+1)],\qquad \mu(G)\ge v/(4p),\qquad w^2\ge2^{57}dp,$$ and there is $R\subseteq T$ such that $$4t/u\le|R|\le16t/u,
\qquad |R|\ge p,$$ and $$|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>\deg_G(L)/2$$ for every $L\in T$.
NOTES: This is the asymmetric cardinality sampler; its enclosing audit verdict is `revise`.
NODE IE-T-ASYMMETRIC-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-ASYMMETRIC-SAMPLE
Under the hypotheses of `IE-L-ASYMMETRIC-SAMPLE`, there exists one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ such that $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge v/(800p).$$
NOTES: This packages interpolation, extension, cleanup, pruning, and recovery from the asymmetric sample.
NODE IE-T-POPULARITY-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-T-ASYMMETRIC-DECODE
If $$u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d,$$ then one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ satisfies $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\epsilon/12800.$$
NOTES: No universal tail estimate strong enough for an exponent above $1/3$ is asserted.
NODE IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY [obligation/open] deps=IE-D-RANK-REACH,IE-D-POPULARITY-TAIL
For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$ there exist $C_\theta,K_\theta\ge1$ and $a_\theta,c_\theta>0$ such that, if $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\rho^{1-\theta},
\qquad u_*\epsilon^2<2^{70}d,$$ then either $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\theta\epsilon,$$ or there exist $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$, $R\subseteq T$, and an integer $d\le D\le K_\theta d$ such that $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.
NOTES: This is the principal unresolved incidence obligation. The direct branch is essential because label-blind reaching families can be large even for perfectly decodable tables.
NODE IE-R-FULL-DICHOTOMY [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY,IE-T-POPULARITY-DECODE
With $c_\theta^*=\min\{c_\theta,1/12800\}$, the high-tail theorem and residual dichotomy imply that $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\rho^{1-\theta}$$ yields either $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\theta^*\epsilon$$ or a rank-reaching $(G,R,D)$ with $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,
\qquad d\le D\le K_\theta d.$$
NOTES: This node aligns the residual incidence obligation with the unconditional interface used by the peer roadmaps.
NODE IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-R-FULL-DICHOTOMY
Enlarge $C_\theta$ until $C_\theta\ge16K_\theta/a_\theta$. In the structured branch, $$D/p\le(K_\theta/C_\theta)\epsilon\le a_\theta\epsilon/16,
\qquad D<p,$$ and therefore $$(D/p)^2+3D/p\le a_\theta\epsilon/4.$$
NOTES: Increasing $C_\theta$ preserves the dichotomy and introduces no hypothesis beyond $d<p$.
NODE IE-L-RANK-INTERP [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE
In the structured branch there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0\quad(M\in R).$$
NOTES: Radicalization preserves every selected graph identity; $|R|>D$ rules out a base-only relation after $D<p$ is known.
NODE IE-L-RANK-EXTEND [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-RANK-INTERP
For every $L\in T$, the more than $D$ points in $\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)$ are roots of the degree-at-most-$D$ polynomial $A(L(t),P_L(t))$. Hence $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0\qquad(L\in T).$$
NOTES: The cited accepted step supplies the same degree-versus-distinct-roots argument.
NODE IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-RANK-EXTEND
Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content in $Z$, $B$ is primitive in $Z$, $h=\deg H$, and $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}B$. Then $B$ is squarefree and $Z$-dependent, $$d\le\delta,
\qquad h+\delta\le D<pd,
\qquad \gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$ At most $h$ line vertices satisfy $H(L(t))\equiv0$; every other line vertex satisfies $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
NOTES: Content removal and vertical separability are kept explicit before resultant counting.
NODE IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE
Let $r=\deg_ZB$. The number of content-trivial or identically $B_Z$-ramified graph lines is at most $$h+(2r-1)\delta-dr^2\le h+\delta^2/d-\delta\le D^2/d-D.$$ On every other graph line, $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))$ is nonzero of degree at most $\delta-d\le D-d$. Deleting every nonsimple accepted incidence therefore costs at most $$(D^2/d-D)/[p(p+1)]+(D-d)/p\le(D/p)^2+D/p.$$
NOTES: The formula contains only literal TeX backslashes and no control characters.
NODE IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN [reduction/drafting] deps=IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT
After vertical cleanup, iteratively delete every point or line vertex of current degree at most $D$. Peeling costs less than $2D/p$. A nonempty $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ remains with $$e(G_1)\ge a_\theta\epsilon/2,
\qquad u(G_1)>D,
\qquad v(G_1)>D.$$ Every $L\in T_1$ satisfies $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and every $x\in X_1$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
NOTES: Every peeled edge is charged once to the removed vertex; the two shore minima are stated explicitly.
NODE IE-L-COMPONENT-LIFT [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN
For every connected component $\Gamma$ of $G_1$, there is $Q_\Gamma\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q_\Gamma\le d$ such that $$B(X,Y,Q_\Gamma(X,Y))\equiv0,
\qquad Q_\Gamma(x)=f(x)\quad(x\in X(\Gamma)).$$
NOTES: The generic implication is exact-audited in a source with enclosing verdict `revise`; it replaces the former seed-factor obligation on the primary route.
NODE IE-L-TEMPLATE-COMPRESS [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-L-COMPONENT-LIFT
Let $\mathcal Q=\{Q_\Gamma:\Gamma\text{ a component of }G_1\}$. For every $Q\in\mathcal Q$, $$B(X,Y,Q)=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad Z-Q(X,Y)\mid B(X,Y,Z).$$ Distinct $Q$ give distinct monic linear divisors, so $$1\le|\mathcal Q|\le\deg_ZB\le\left\lfloor D/d\right\rfloor\le K_\theta.$$
NOTES: This is the polynomial remainder theorem followed by distinct linear-divisor counting.
NODE IE-R-COMPONENT-AGGREGATE [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-TEMPLATE-COMPRESS
Partition $E_1$ by the value of $Q_\Gamma$. Some $Q\in\mathcal Q$ carries an edge set $E_Q$ with $$|E_Q|/[p^2(p+1)]\ge e(G_1)/|\mathcal Q|\ge a_\theta\epsilon/(2K_\theta).$$ If $X_Q$ is the union of its component point sets, then $Q=f$ on $X_Q$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge|X_Q|/p^2\ge|E_Q|/[p^2(p+1)]\ge a_\theta\epsilon/(2K_\theta).$$
NOTES: Components producing the same polynomial are combined before the edge pigeonhole.
NODE IE-R-CLOSE [reduction/blocked] deps=IE-R-COMPONENT-AGGREGATE
For fixed $\eta>0$, set $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. Since $0<\rho<1$, $$\rho^{1-\eta}\ge\rho^{1-\theta}.$$ The high-tail, residual direct, and structured branches yield constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\rho^{1-\eta}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES: The only genuinely new mathematical bottleneck on this path is the residual bounded-weight dichotomy; downstream drafting nodes still require the harness's exact audits.
NODE IE-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=IE-R-CLOSE
For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, integer $100<d<p$, function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line table, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES: The endpoint recovers one polynomial, not merely a list. Work state does not assert proof verification.
NODE IE-L-POPULAR-CORE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
If an accepted carrier $G$ has $e(G)=\delta>0$ and $\tau(G)\le\sigma$, put $k_0=\lfloor\delta p/100\rfloor$. There is $G_0\subseteq G$ such that $$e(G_0)\ge\delta/4,
\qquad u(G_0),v(G_0)\ge k_0,
\qquad \delta/4\le\tau(G_0)\le\sigma.$$
NOTES: This preserves the useful round-2 constant-mass core.
NODE IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-RANK-REACH
Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be connected with $u(G),v(G)\ge k$, $t=|T|$, and $\tau=\tau(G)$. If $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,
\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\mu(G),\tau\ge k/(4p),$$ and there is $R\subseteq T$ such that $$4t/k\le|R|\le16t/k,
\qquad |\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>\deg_G(L)/2$$ for every $L\in T$.
NOTES: Both inputs have independently accepted audits and give the cardinality-based baseline.
NODE IE-T-COMP-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE
Under the hypotheses of `IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE`, there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ such that $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge k/(800p).$$
NOTES: This is the accepted component-level recovery theorem retained from round 2.
NODE IE-R-SQRT-CEILING [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE
If $0<k\le C\epsilon p$, $\tau\ge k/(4p)$, and $$k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\epsilon^2\ge[C_*/(4C^2)](d/p).$$
NOTES: Thus component localization alone cannot reach the near-linear exponent.
NODE IE-L-TEMPLATE-POPULARITY [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
Let disjoint line families $T_1,\ldots,T_r$ carry normalized accepted mass $\delta$ and total line measure $\tau$, and suppose $$P_L(t)=Q_j(L(t))\qquad(L\in T_j)$$ for total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomials $Q_j$. If $\alpha=\max_j\Pr_x[Q_j(x)=f(x)]$, then $$\delta\le\alpha\tau+\sqrt{r\alpha\tau/(p+1)}.$$ Hence $r\le\delta(p+1)$ implies $\alpha\ge\delta/4$. If all labels are constant and $\mu_0=\max_a\Pr_x[f(x)=a]$, then $$\delta\le\mu_0\tau+\sqrt{\tau/(p+1)},$$ so $\delta\ge2\sqrt{\tau/(p+1)}$ implies $\mu_0\ge\delta/(2\tau)\ge\delta/2$.
NOTES: These lemmas consume an already extracted structured family; they do not create the templates.
NODE IE-L-INTEGRAL-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
For $p\ge103$ and $2\le K\le p/(100\log p)$ there is a legal table with $$1/(2K)\le\epsilon\le2/K$$ such that every line subset reaching half of every accepted line has $$|R|\ge Kp/16\ge p/(32\epsilon),$$ while $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\epsilon/2.$$
NOTES: This refutes a universal small graph-only cover, not soundness.
NODE IE-L-STAR-ENVELOPES [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
Let $a_x$ and $b_L$ be the accepted point and line degrees. For $\mu=k+\lambda$, where $k=\lfloor\mu\rfloor$ and $0\le\lambda<1$, put $$J_s(\mu)=(1-\lambda)(k)_s+\lambda(k+1)_s.$$ Then $$\Gamma_s^P=\mathbb E_x[(a_x)_s]/(p+1)_s\ge J_s((p+1)\epsilon)/(p+1)_s\qquad(0\le s\le p+1),$$ $$\Gamma_s^L=\mathbb E_L[(b_L)_s]/(p)_s\ge J_s(p\epsilon)/(p)_s\qquad(0\le s\le p),$$ and the with-replacement moments satisfy $\widetilde\Gamma_r^P,\widetilde\Gamma_r^L\ge\epsilon^r$ for $r\ge1$. In particular, $$\Gamma_2^P=\epsilon^2-\epsilon(1-\epsilon)/p+\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)/[p(p+1)].$$
NOTES: The star order ranges incorporate the verifier correction; no unqualified $\Theta(\epsilon^2)$ claim is made near $\epsilon=1/p$.
NODE IE-L-REGULAR-CARRIER [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-STAR-ENVELOPES
There is a connected, full-support legal table with $\epsilon_0=d/p$, every line of accepted degree $d$, exactly $pd$ points of accepted degree $d+1$, every other point of degree $d$, and $\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=1$. It attains both star envelopes in `IE-L-STAR-ENVELOPES`. For $0<\vartheta\le1$, let $\operatorname{OPT}_\vartheta$ be the minimum total nonnegative line weight giving every accepted line at least $\vartheta d$ capped coverage through other accepted lines. Then $$\vartheta p(p+1)/(d+1)\le\operatorname{OPT}_\vartheta\le\vartheta p(p+1)/(d-1)=\Theta(\vartheta p/\epsilon_0).$$
NOTES: The enclosing audit verdict is `revise`; the corrected statement concerns the optimum, not every feasible weighting.
NODE IE-L-STAR-ENERGY-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-STAR-ENVELOPES
For every fixed integer $S\ge2$ and all sufficiently large primes $p$, there is a legal constant-label instance with $d=101$ such that $$\epsilon=\Theta(\log p/[p\log\log p]),
\qquad \operatorname{Agr}_{101}(f)<2/p,$$ every affine line supports acceptance, and $$\Gamma_s^P\le2^{s+2}(S-1)\epsilon^s\qquad(2\le s\le S).$$
NOTES: These examples lie below every fixed target trigger and therefore do not refute near-linear soundness.
NODE IE-L-AUXILIARY-DRC [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-STAR-ENVELOPES
Every raw accepted affine incidence graph is $K_{2,2}$-free. Let $H$ be the graph on the $N=p(p+1)$ affine lines, joining distinct nonparallel lines when both accept their intersection. With $\mu_0=(p+1)\epsilon$ and average degree $\bar\Delta$, $$e(H)=\sum_x\binom{a_x}{2},
\qquad \bar\Delta\ge p\mu_0(\mu_0-1)/(p+1).$$ For an integer $1\le t\le\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor$, put $$M_t(H)=\frac{\sum_L\binom{\deg_H(L)}t}{\binom Nt}.$$ Some $t$-line seed has at least $$M_t(H)\ge N\binom{\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor}t/\binom Nt$$ common neighbors; if $\mu_0\ge2$ and $\bar\Delta\ge2t+2$, then $M_t(H)\ge N(\epsilon^2/4)^t$. For every integer $1\le h\le t$, at least $$M_t(H)-\binom t2(p+1)/(t-h+1)$$ common neighbors meet the seed lines in at least $h$ distinct points.
NOTES: Raw $K_{2,2}$-freeness is recorded but is not transferred to the auxiliary graph.
NODE IE-R-DRC-BARRIER [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-AUXILIARY-DRC
Put $$D_t=16(\lfloor\sqrt{dt}\rfloor+1).$$ A transfer requiring $h>D_t$ with $h\le t$ requires $t>D_t$ and hence $t>256d$. For fixed $d,t,C$ and $$\epsilon_p=C(d/p)^{1/3},$$ $$N(\epsilon_p^2/4)^t=O_{C,d,t}(p^{2-2t/3})\longrightarrow0.$$ If $t>D_t$ and $h=D_t+1$, substituting this density term into the concurrency-corrected bound of `IE-L-AUXILIARY-DRC` is eventually negative and therefore supplies no root-rich target.
NOTES: This limits only the displayed density-only certificate; it is not an impossibility result for algebra-aware DRC.
NODE IE-L-SINGULAR-SEED-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
If $d\ge101$ and $p>4d+1$, there is an absolutely irreducible $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree $D=2d$ supporting $(p-1)/2>D$ distinct degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines, while no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ satisfies $$C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0.$$ Every vertically ramified lift on those graph lines is singular on $C=0$.
NOTES: This accepted obstruction explains why the $B_Z\ne0$ cleanup hypothesis cannot be discarded.
MSG reply -> algebraic-engine refs=researcher-0001:P7.1,end-to-end-soundness:E2E-21,end-to-end-soundness:E2E-22,end-to-end-soundness:E2E-23
`IE-L-COMPONENT-LIFT` imports the generic whole-relation lift from `researcher-0001:P7.1`. For each component, $B(X,Y,Q_\Gamma)=0$ gives $Z-Q_\Gamma\mid B$; hence at most $\deg_ZB\le D/d$ distinct component polynomials occur, and edge pigeonholing yields one global $Q$. This adopts `E2E-21`--`E2E-23` and removes the former irreducible-factor seed obligation from the primary path; simple-root cleanup remains essential.
MSG reply -> end-to-end-soundness refs=researcher-0010:[P8].1,incidence-engine:IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY,incidence-engine:IE-R-FULL-DICHOTOMY
Researcher-0010 `[P8]` handles $u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d$. Accordingly, `IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY` is explicitly the complementary low-tail obligation, while `IE-R-FULL-DICHOTOMY` exports the unconditional decoder-or-rank-reaching interface needed by the other roadmaps. No source proves the residual $D\le K_\theta d$ statement.
MSG objection -> global refs=researcher-0009:P1.1,researcher-0009:P5.1,researcher-0009:P6.1,researcher-0009:P10.1,researcher-0009:P11.1
Researcher-0009 strengthens the graph-only warning: one connected full-support carrier simultaneously attains every point- and line-star lower envelope, has fractional reaching optimum $\Theta(\vartheta p/\epsilon)$, and has the perfect decoder $Q=0$. Its auxiliary DRC estimate must also pay the concurrency correction in `IE-L-AUXILIARY-DRC`; at the cubic scale the resulting density-only root-rich bound is trivial. These are method barriers, not counterexamples to the target.
ROADMAP end-to-end-soundness ROUND 3
SUMMARY: Round 3 retains the independently accepted KTZ component chain `E2E-04`–`E2E-12`, whose available unconditional machinery still stops at a conditional square-root frontier. The near-linear spine is `E2E-15`–`E2E-24`: a label-aware rank-or-decode dichotomy must retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ mass while producing weighted degree $D=O_\theta(d)$. The rank-reaching definition now correctly allows $D\ge d$, with $D<p$ derived downstream. The main structural simplification is `E2E-21`–`E2E-23`: lift the common cleaned relation separately on every connected component, observe that each resulting $Q_\Gamma$ contributes the divisor $Z-Q_\Gamma$ of the same polynomial $B$, and hence bound the number of distinct component polynomials by $\deg_ZB\le D/d$. Elementary edge pigeonholing then yields one global polynomial across all components, avoiding both Vinh mixing and the former standalone irreducible seed-factor lift. The target remains blocked on `E2E-15` and standalone audits of the generalized algebraic transfers; the uniform $Cd/p$ endpoint remains separately refuted.
NODE E2E-01 [definition/candidate] deps=
Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\deg P_L\le d$ for every affine line $L$. Define $$\rho=\frac d p,\qquad \epsilon=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],\qquad \operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)].$$
NOTES: The scope is exactly $m=2$, prime field, and integer $100<d<p$.
NODE E2E-02 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-01
Let $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(x,L):x\in L,\ P_L(x)=f(x)\}.$$ For an accepted subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$ put $$e(G)=\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)}.$$ For $c>0$, let $\mathsf{Done}_c$ mean that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ satisfies $\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c\epsilon$.
NOTES: The normalization converts edge mass directly into a lower bound on point agreement.
NODE E2E-03 [reduction/candidate] deps=E2E-01
For fixed $\eta>0$, put $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. Since $0<\rho<1$, $$0<\theta\le\frac14,\qquad \rho^{1-\eta}\ge\rho^{1-\theta}.$$
NOTES: It is enough to establish the engine for $0<\theta\le1/4$.
NODE E2E-04 [literature_input/candidate] deps=E2E-02
Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a connected accepted graph of minimum degree $k$, and put $\tau=|T|/[p(p+1)]$. If $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\frac{|X|}{p^2}\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.$$
NOTES: This begins the independently accepted component-level KTZ chain.
NODE E2E-05 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-04
Under `E2E-04`, there is $R\subseteq T$ such that $$\frac{4|T|}{k}\le|R|\le\frac{16|T|}{k},\qquad |R|\ge p+1,$$ and every $L\in T$ has more than half of its accepted neighbors incident to a line of $R\setminus\{L\}$.
NOTES: The selector is cardinality-based and local to one connected component.
NODE E2E-06 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-05
Put $$D=16\left(\left\lfloor\sqrt{d|R|}\right\rfloor+1\right).$$ There is a nonzero squarefree $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ with $\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D$, $A_Z\not\equiv0$, and $$A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0\qquad(M\in R).$$ Moreover, $D/k<1/5000$ and $D<k\le p<|R|$.
NOTES: The interpolation degree depends on the cardinality of the reaching family.
NODE E2E-07 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-06
For every $L\in T$, $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
NOTES: More than $D$ reached roots force each remaining restriction identity.
NODE E2E-08 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-07
Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content in $Z$. Then $B$ is squarefree and has total degree below $p$. There is a formal directional derivative $\mathcal D$ with $\gcd(B,\mathcal DB)=1$ and an exceptional set satisfying $$|E_{\rm exc}|\le pD^2+D|T|.$$ Outside $E_{\rm exc}$, the $B$-identity holds and $\mathcal DB$ is nonzero at every retained accepted incidence.
NOTES: The resultant argument is valid throughout the campaign's prime-field range.
NODE E2E-09 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-08
One has $$\frac{|E_{\rm exc}|}{|E|}<\frac1{4000}.$$ Peeling vertices below $k_1=\lfloor k/100\rfloor$ leaves a nonempty accepted graph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $k_1$.
NOTES: Connectivity may fragment during the peeling operation.
NODE E2E-10 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-09
Every point vertex $x$ of $G_1$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
NOTES: Two retained directions convert generic derivative nonvanishing into vertical simplicity.
NODE E2E-11 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-10
Under `E2E-04`, there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
NOTES: The conclusion is one polynomial, not a list.
NODE E2E-12 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-11
Let $k_0=\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$. If a component of the KTZ-pruned core has line measure $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$ and $$\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\cdot2^{40}\Lambda}\left(\frac d p\right)^{1/2},$$ then one degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/160000$.
NOTES: Local agreement does not itself imply the required small-component hypothesis.
NODE E2E-13 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-02
There are legal constant-label tables with $\epsilon\asymp1/K$ for which every family reaching half of every accepted line has size $\Omega(p/\epsilon)$ although a constant polynomial has agreement $\Omega(\epsilon)$. Moreover, at $\epsilon=d/p$ there is a connected full-support legal table with perfect decoder $Q=0$, raw accepted graph $K_{2,2}$-free, and fractional reaching optimum $$\frac{\vartheta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_{\vartheta}\le\frac{\vartheta p(p+1)}{d-1}=\Theta\!\left(\frac{\vartheta p}{\epsilon}\right).$$
NOTES: These examples have immediate decoders and therefore force, rather than refute, the decode branch in `E2E-15`.
NODE E2E-14 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-02
For an integer $D\ge d$, put $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\},$$ $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching when $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<\dim V_D$, and every $L\in T$ has more than $D$ accepted neighbors $x$ for which some $M\in R\setminus\{L\}$ also satisfies $(x,M)\in E$.
NOTES: Unlike the round-2 definition, this does not assume $D<p$; that inequality is derived at `E2E-16`.
NODE E2E-15 [obligation/open] deps=E2E-03,E2E-14
For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, there exist $C_\theta,K_\theta\ge1$ and $a_\theta,c_\theta>0$ such that, whenever $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\rho^{1-\theta},$$ either $\mathsf{Done}_{c_\theta}$ holds, or there is a rank-reaching tuple $(G,R,D)$ satisfying $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,\qquad D\le K_\theta d.$$
NOTES: This is the principal open incidence bottleneck. The direct-decoder branch is indispensable by `E2E-13`.
NODE E2E-16 [reduction/candidate] deps=E2E-15
Enlarge $C_\theta$ until $K_\theta/C_\theta\le a_\theta/16$. In the relation branch, $$\frac{D}{\epsilon p}\le\frac{K_\theta}{C_\theta}\rho^\theta\le\frac{a_\theta}{16}.$$ Consequently, $$D<p,\qquad \left(\frac Dp\right)^2+3\frac Dp\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{2}.$$
NOTES: Increasing $C_\theta$ is harmless; if the trigger exceeds one, the implication is vacuous.
NODE E2E-17 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-16
For the rank-reaching tuple, there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0\quad(M\in R).$$ Indeed, take the squarefree radical of a nonzero kernel element; $|R|>D$ excludes a $Z$-independent kernel element, and $D<p$ makes every nonconstant $Z$-dependence separable.
NOTES: The abstract rank-kernel formulation still needs a standalone exact audit.
NODE E2E-18 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-17
For every line vertex $L\in T$, $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
NOTES: This is the accepted reached-root argument with the rank-reaching interface substituted for the cardinality selector.
NODE E2E-19 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-18
Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content in $Z$. Then $B$ is squarefree, primitive and $Z$-dependent, with $\operatorname{wdeg}B\le D<p$ and $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. There is $E_{\rm exc}\subseteq E$ such that $$|E_{\rm exc}|\le pD^2+D|T|.$$ Every line incident to $E\setminus E_{\rm exc}$ satisfies $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and every retained edge $(x,L)$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
NOTES: The bounded-weight gate makes the exact $O(D/p)$ cleanup loss absorbable below $\epsilon$.
NODE E2E-20 [reduction/drafting] deps=E2E-19
Delete $E_{\rm exc}$ and iteratively delete every point or line vertex of current degree at most $D$. The resulting nonempty accepted graph $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ satisfies $$e(G_1)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2}\epsilon,\qquad \operatorname{mindeg}(G_1)>D.$$ Every $L\in T_1$ satisfies $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and every $x\in X_1$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
NOTES: Exceptional deletion costs at most $(D/p)^2+D/p$; peeling both shores at degree $D$ costs less than $2D/p$.
NODE E2E-21 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-20
For every connected component $\Gamma$ of $G_1$, there is $Q_\Gamma\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q_\Gamma\le d$ such that $$B(X,Y,Q_\Gamma(X,Y))\equiv0,
\qquad Q_\Gamma(x)=f(x)\quad(x\in X(\Gamma)).$$
NOTES: The generic component interface is exact-audited but belongs to a source with overall revise verdict; a fresh standalone audit remains needed.
NODE E2E-22 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-21
Let $\mathcal Q$ be the set of distinct polynomials $Q_\Gamma$ from `E2E-21`. For every $Q\in\mathcal Q$, the identity $B(X,Y,Q)=0$ implies $$Z-Q(X,Y)\mid B(X,Y,Z).$$ Distinct $Q$ give distinct monic irreducible divisors, so $$1\le|\mathcal Q|\le\deg_ZB\le\left\lfloor\frac Dd\right\rfloor\le K_\theta.$$
NOTES: This elementary remainder-theorem argument replaces the former heavy-factor and simple-seed factor-linearization nodes.
NODE E2E-23 [reduction/drafting] deps=E2E-22
Partition $E_1$ by the polynomial $Q_\Gamma$ assigned to its component. Some $Q\in\mathcal Q$ carries normalized edge mass at least $$\frac{e(G_1)}{|\mathcal Q|}\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$ If $X_Q$ is the union of its component point sets and $E_Q$ the corresponding edges, then $Q=f$ on $X_Q$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{|X_Q|}{p^2}\ge\frac{|E_Q|}{p^2(p+1)}\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$ Together with the direct branch of `E2E-15`, this proves $\mathsf{Done}_{c_\theta^*}$ for $$c_\theta^*=\min\left\{c_\theta,\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\right\}.$$
NOTES: Disconnected components sharing the same component lift are charged to the same algebraic polynomial.
NODE E2E-24 [theorem/blocked] deps=E2E-23
For every fixed $\eta>0$, there are $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\rho^{1-\eta}$$ implies that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES: All constants depend only on the fixed exponent parameter.
NODE E2E-25 [obligation/refuted] deps=E2E-01
For every absolute $C,c>0$, there are arbitrarily large primes $p$ and legal instances with $d=101$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C\frac d p,
\qquad \operatorname{Agr}_d(f)<c\epsilon.$$
NOTES: This does not refute any fixed-$\eta$ trigger because the counterexamples lie below $(d/p)^{1-\eta}$.
NODE E2E-26 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-01
For $p>4d+1$ and $D=2d$, there is an absolutely irreducible $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree $D$ supporting more than $D$ distinct degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines, while no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ satisfies $C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$. Every vertically ramified lift on the supported graphs is singular.
NOTES: The intentionally refuted bundle in researcher-0006:P12 is not used as positive evidence. This node shows why `E2E-19` must certify simple roots.
NODE E2E-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=E2E-24
For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every legal bivariate line table, $$\operatorname{Agr}_{\rm LVP}(f,P)\ge C_\eta\left(\frac d p\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies that there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\operatorname{Agr}_{\rm LVP}(f,P).$$
NOTES: The principal mathematical bottleneck is `E2E-15`; the generalized rank-kernel, cleanup-transfer, and component-template chain also awaits standalone exact audits. Work states do not assert proof status.
MSG reply -> algebraic-engine refs=end-to-end-soundness:E2E-21,end-to-end-soundness:E2E-22,end-to-end-soundness:E2E-23,researcher-0001:P7.1,algebraic-engine:AE-16,algebraic-engine:AE-19
Round 3 can bypass the standalone seed-factor lift in `AE-19`. After `E2E-20`, apply the generic whole-relation lift componentwise. Every resulting $Q_\Gamma$ satisfies $B(X,Y,Q_\Gamma)=0$, so $Z-Q_\Gamma$ divides the common $B$; hence there are at most $\deg_ZB\le D/d$ distinct component polynomials. Edge pigeonholing then gives one global polynomial. Please audit `E2E-21`–`E2E-23` as a standalone combined lemma.

`E2E-15` remains the principal incidence obligation: retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ accepted mass while producing a rank-reaching relation with $D\le K_\theta d$, or decode directly. The connected carrier of researcher-0009 has optimal fractional reaching cost $\Theta(\vartheta p/\epsilon)$ and a $K_{2,2}$-free raw graph despite the perfect decoder $Q=0$, so connectivity, moments, fractional covers, and raw DRC cannot replace the label-aware alternative.

The bound $D=O_\theta(d)$ now has a cleaner second use. Besides making the cleanup loss $O_\theta(d/p)$ absorbable, it bounds the number of distinct component lifts by $\deg_ZB\le D/d=O_\theta(1)$. Thus `E2E-21`–`E2E-23` aggregate one polynomial by elementary edge pigeonholing and no longer require a globally heavy irreducible factor or a separate simple-seed factor-linearization theorem.

TITLE=Integrated bivariate prime-field synthesis: a conditional 1−o(1) route with one open incidence lemma

THEOREM=
GLOBAL=
STEP P1 [conjectural] deps=
STATEMENT: Assume RDθ with constants Cθ,Kθ,aθ,cθ and Cθ≥8Kθ/aθ.
PROOF: This is the only assumed statement. Formal rank deficiency means rank Φ_{R,D}<dim V_D, not merely failure of surjectivity. The graph lies inside Eacc, R⊆T, line parametrizations are affine bijections, and all reached points are distinct.
STEP P2 [proved] deps=P1
STATEMENT: Every structured RDθ branch satisfies D<p and has enough budget for cleanup and peeling.
PROOF: Put ρ=d/p and x=D/p. From ε≥Cθρ^(1−θ) and D≤Kθd, x≤(Kθ/Cθ)ερ^θ≤aθ ε/8≤1/8. Thus D<p and x²+3x≤25x/8≤25aθ ε/64<aθ ε/2.
STEP P3 [proved] deps=P1;P2
STATEMENT: There is a nonzero squarefree Z-dependent A of weighted degree at most D vanishing formally on every selected graph M∈R.
PROOF: Rank deficiency gives a nonzero A0 in ker Φ. Replace A0 by its squarefree radical A. Every selected-line identity survives because F_p[t] is a domain, and weighted degree does not increase. If A were Z-independent, each of the more than D distinct lines in R would divide a nonzero polynomial of total degree at most D, impossible. Hence A depends on Z. Since deg_Z A<p, A_Z is nonzero.
STEP P4 [proved] deps=P1;P3
STATEMENT: A vanishes formally on every line graph indexed by T.
PROOF: For a reached point x=ell_L(t)=ell_M(s), acceptedness gives P_L(t)=f(x)=P_M(s). The selected identity on M therefore gives A(x,P_L(t))=0. Each L has more than D distinct such points, while A(ell_L(t),P_L(t)) has degree at most D. Root counting forces the formal identity on L.
STEP P5 [proved] deps=P3;P4
STATEMENT: Outside at most D content lines, a primitive squarefree Z-dependent B of weighted degree at most D vanishes on every target graph.
PROOF: Write A=HB, where H∈F_p[X,Y] is the gcd of the Z-coefficients and B is primitive. Squarefreeness of A makes B squarefree; A_Z=HB_Z≠0 makes B Z-dependent. If H does not vanish identically on L, the identity H|_L·B(ell_L,P_L)=0 cancels in the domain F_p[t]. At most deg H≤D lines are content lines.
STEP P6 [proved] deps=P2;P5
STATEMENT: Direct vertical cleanup leaves only incidences satisfying B(x,f(x))=0 and B_Z(x,f(x))≠0.
PROOF: If an irreducible C divides both B and B_Z, squarefreeness forces C_Z=0. Since deg_Z C<p, C is Z-independent, contradicting primitivity; hence gcd(B,B_Z)=1. KTZ Lemma 2.6 bounds identically B_Z-ramified graph lines by D(D−1). Including content lines gives fewer than D² bad lines. On every other line, B_Z(ell_L,P_L) is a nonzero polynomial of degree at most D−d≤D. Deleting all nonsimple accepted incidences therefore costs less than (D/p)²+D/p.
STEP P7 [proved] deps=P1;P2;P6
STATEMENT: A clean minimum-degree graph retaining at least half the certificate mass exists.
PROOF: Iteratively delete each current point or line vertex of degree at most D. Every edge is charged once, and the total normalized peeling cost is below 2D/p. Together with P6, total loss is below (D/p)²+3D/p<aθ ε/2. Thus a nonempty graph G1 remains with e(G1)≥aθ ε/2 and minimum degree greater than D.
STEP P8 [proved] deps=P2;P7
STATEMENT: Every connected component Γ of G1 supplies QΓ of total degree at most d with B(X,Y,QΓ)=0 and QΓ=f throughout Γ.
PROOF: In a connected component Γ, choose a point b. More than D distinct incident lines pass through the simple root (b,f(b)). The characteristic-free formal implicit recursion, dividing only by B_Z(b,f(b)), gives a unique two-variable branch. Truncate it at total degree d to obtain QΓ. On every incident line this truncation equals P_L, so B(X,Y,QΓ) has total degree at most D and vanishes on more than D distinct lines; hence it is zero. Along an edge, QΓ and P_L are two formal roots with the same simple initial value, so uniqueness makes them identical. Induction through Γ gives QΓ=f on every point vertex. This is the mechanism of KTZ Lemmas 2.5, 2.7, 6.1, and 6.3.
STEP P9 [proved] deps=P5;P8
STATEMENT: All component polynomials form a list of size at most Kθ.
PROOF: Polynomial division by the monic polynomial Z−QΓ shows that B(X,Y,QΓ)=0 is equivalent to Z−QΓ dividing B. Distinct QΓ give distinct monic irreducible divisors. Consequently the number of distinct component templates is at most deg_Z B≤wdeg(B)/d≤D/d≤Kθ.
STEP P10 [proved] deps=P7;P8;P9
STATEMENT: The structured branch returns one Q with agreement at least aθ ε/(2Kθ).
PROOF: Pigeonhole the edges of G1 by their component template. One Q carries normalized edge mass at least aθ ε/(2Kθ). It equals f on every point incident to those edges. Since each point has at most p+1 incident affine lines, its point agreement is at least the same normalized edge mass.
STEP P11 [conditional] deps=P1;P10
STATEMENT: Under RDθ, agreement is at least min(cθ,aθ/(2Kθ))ε.
PROOF: The direct RDθ branch gives cθ ε; P10 gives aθ ε/(2Kθ) in the structured branch. Taking their minimum proves the claimed fixed-θ conditional theorem.
STEP P12 [conditional] deps=P11
STATEMENT: Assuming RDθ for every θ∈(0,1/4], the threshold exponent is 1−η for every fixed η∈(0,1).
PROOF: For η∈(0,1), take θ=min(η,1/4). Since 0<d/p<1, the η-hypothesis implies the θ-hypothesis. Rename the constants from P11.

TITLE=The Bivariate Prime-Field Cubic Bottleneck: Support Sensitivity and a Modal Obstruction
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=For prime p, 101≤d<p, and any accepted carrier of edge mass δ supported on a σ-fraction of affine lines, δ³≥10^15dσ/p implies a total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with global agreement at least δ/8000. Under σ≤Aδ this yields a conditional exponent-1/2 threshold. Moreover, for d=101 and all sufficiently large primes there are modal constant line labels with ε=Θ(log p/(p log log p)) but Agr_101(f)<2/p; consequently no uniform trigger C d/p can guarantee global agreement cε for fixed positive C,c.
GLOBAL=The bivariate KTZ exponent 1/3 is not improved unconditionally. Its exact source is the combination |R|=O(σp/δ), D=Θ(sqrt(d|R|)), and the later requirement D=O(δp), yielding δ³=Ω(dσ/p). Retaining σ proves a conditional exponent-1/2 theorem when σ=O(δ), and prime-field differentiation can be simplified to B_Z. Separately, modal constant line labels at the legal boundary d=101 rigorously refute any uniform exact threshold C d/p with global agreement Ω(ε). Fixed-η near-linear soundness remains open, but any exponent-1 formulation must accommodate an occupancy factor of order log p/log log p for fixed d.
STEP P1 [proved] deps=Uniform incidence normalization
STATEMENT: Prune an accepted carrier to minimum degree k0=floor(δp/100) without losing a power of δ.
PROOF: Iterative deletion removes at most p²k0 point-side edges and p(p+1)k0 line-side edges, hence normalized loss below δ/50. The surviving graph has minimum degree k0, edge mass at least δ/4, and line support at most σ.
STEP P2 [proved] deps=P1;Hoeffding inequality;Chebyshev inequality
STATEMENT: Construct a support-sensitive selected line set.
PROOF: Independent selection at rate 8/k0 covers half the neighbors of every retained line except with union probability below 1/4. Chebyshev controls the selected size with failure below 1/4, yielding a deterministic R with 100(p+1)≤|R|≤6400σp/δ.
STEP P3 [proved] deps=P2;KTZ Lemma 2.2
STATEMENT: Interpolate the selected line graphs at weighted degree D=O(sqrt(dσp/δ)).
PROOF: With D=16(floor(sqrt(d|R|))+1), dim(V_D)≥D³/(64d)>|R|(D+1). The hypothesis bounds D by δp/10000, hence below p,k0/2,k1/2; minimal weighted degree gives a squarefree Z-dependent interpolant.
STEP P4 [proved] deps=P2;P3
STATEMENT: Extend the interpolation identity and remove coefficient content safely.
PROOF: Coverage supplies more than D distinct roots on every retained line, forcing its graph identity. Factoring A=HB and excluding at most D lines where H vanishes identically transfers the identity to primitive B in F_p[t], without pointwise division.
STEP P5 [proved] deps=P3;P4
STATEMENT: A squarefree primitive polynomial of total degree below p is coprime to its vertical derivative.
PROOF: Every irreducible factor of primitive B depends on Z. Its Z-degree is positive and below p, so its derivative is nonzero and not divisible by that factor. Reduction modulo each distinct factor proves gcd(B,B_Z)=1.
STEP P6 [proved] deps=P5;KTZ Lemma 2.6
STATEMENT: Delete all degenerate roots with controlled loss.
PROOF: The content, resultant-degenerate, and bad-point deletions cost at most D/[p(p+1)]+D(D−1)/[p(p+1)]+D/p<δ/16. Pruning at k1 costs below δ/500 and leaves vertically simple roots and minimum degree k1.
STEP P7 [proved] deps=P6;KTZ Lemma 2.7;KTZ Lemma 6.1
STATEMENT: Lift and propagate one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial on every surviving component.
PROOF: Homogeneous Newton recursion divides only by B_Z(x,f(x)). More than D incident directions kill all lifted homogeneous terms of degrees d+1 through D. The degree-d truncation is an exact polynomial root and simple-root uniqueness propagates it through a component.
STEP P8 [proved] deps=P6;P7;KTZ Lemma 2.1
STATEMENT: Convert a component polynomial into global agreement δ/8000.
PROOF: Minimum degree gives e≥κa/2 and e≥κb; incidence mixing gives e≤ab+p^−1/2 sqrt(ab). Since p^−1/2≤κ/4, assuming either a or b below κ/4 yields a contradiction. Thus component point density is at least δ/8000.
STEP P9 [proved] deps=P2;P3;P6;P7
STATEMENT: Identify the exact cubic exponent ledger.
PROOF: Substitution of |R|=O(σp/δ) into D=Θ(sqrt(d|R|)), followed by D=O(δp), is equivalent to δ³=Ω(dσ/p). The D²/p² and p^−1/2 gates are weaker.
STEP P10 [conditional] deps=P9
STATEMENT: Obtain exponent 1/2 under concentrated line support.
PROOF: Under σ≤Aδ, the cubic relation reduces algebraically to δ²=Ω(Ad/p). Because σ≥δ, the support parameter cannot improve this architecture past exponent 1/2.
STEP P11 [proved] deps=Second-moment inequality
STATEMENT: Lower-bound modal agreement on a random line.
PROOF: For r=floor(log p/(4 log log p)), exact occupancy estimates give E[Y²]≤65E[Y]² for the number of symbols occurring exactly r times. Therefore a line's modal multiplicity has expectation at least r/65.
STEP P12 [proved] deps=P11;Factorial-moment union bound;McDiarmid inequality
STATEMENT: Upper-control the modal local agreement.
PROOF: The maximum occupancy has expectation at most 25r. The global line-average modal agreement has pointwise Lipschitz constant 1/p², giving probability at most exp(−1250r²) of exceeding 50r/p.
STEP P13 [proved] deps=P11;P12;Chernoff inequality
STATEMENT: Construct the deterministic modal table and bound every global polynomial.
PROOF: A fixed polynomial has Bin(p²,1/p) agreements. Union over p^5253 polynomials leaves failure at most exp(−p/4). Intersecting this event with the modal bounds by an expectation argument yields a deterministic table with ε≥r/(130p), ε≤50r/p, and global agreement below 2/p.
STEP P14 [refuted] deps=P13
STATEMENT: Universal exact-linear soundness at threshold C d/p with linear recovery.
PROOF: For any C,c>0, choose a sufficiently large prime with r≥max(130·101C,260/c). Then the modal table satisfies the premise ε≥C·101/p but violates Agr_101(f)≥cε.
STEP P15 [proved] deps=Newton interpolation
STATEMENT: Every point table has global agreement at least (d+1)(d+2)/(2p²).
PROOF: A triangular Cartesian set of (d+1)(d+2)/2 points has an invertible total-degree Newton evaluation matrix. Interpolating f there supplies a degree-at-most-d polynomial agreeing on the entire set.
STEP P16 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: Diffuse accepted support need not imply a soundness obstruction or σ=O(ε).

TITLE=Component-Sensitive Line Concentration and a Sharp Global-Cover Obstruction
STATUS=conditional
THEOREM=Let C_*=2^40. GLOBAL=A component-sensitive concentration theorem is proved: a connected accepted component of minimum degree k and line measure tau decodes when k^2 is sufficiently larger than dp and k^3 is sufficiently larger than d tau p^2, yielding global agreement at least k/(800p). TITLE=Uniform Normal Multiplicity Is Exponent-Neutral in the Bivariate KTZ Interpolation Step
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=For prime p, 100<d<p, distinct selected line graphs R of size n, multiplicity s>=1, and d<=D<p, order-s graph-ideal vanishing imposes at most n C_s(D,d) equations, where C_s(D,d)=sum_{a+b<s} max(D-a-db+1,0). If N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d), with N_d(D)=sum_{j<=D/d} binom(D-dj+2,2), a nonzero interpolant exists. Each compatible transverse intersection supplies an s-fold root, so h such points transfer the identity when sh>D. The explicit choice D_0=floor(4sqrt(dns(s+1)))+1 suffices when d<=D_0<p and D_0<sh. Conversely, in the KTZ regime n>D, any proof using only this raw dimension criterion and root count must satisfy h^2>dn/12. Thus at n=Theta(p/epsilon), h=Theta(epsilon p), uniform multiplicity retains the cubic condition epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).
GLOBAL=A fully quantified transverse-jet interpolation lemma and a rigorous architecture-specific obstruction were proved. TITLE=Hasse-Height Stratification and Factorwise Simple-Root Cleanup
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=For every prime p and integer 101<=d<p, a squarefree Z-dependent interpolant A of weighted degree a, with weights (1,1,d), admits an explicit factorwise cleanup. If A=HB, B=c product_i C_i, Delta=wdeg(B), and iota is the weighted degree of gcd(B,B_Z), then at most h+floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d) content or exceptional graph lines are removed. Every remaining separable-type line loses at most min(p,Delta-d) points, every inseparable-type line at most min(p,Delta-1), and every retained point of graph degree at least two satisfies B_Z nonzero. Each irreducible factor has a p-power Hasse height s with weighted degree at least ds, and its height-s stratum obeys the common-graph bound (2r_s-s)Delta_s-d r_s^2. In particular, Delta<pd implies gcd(B,B_Z)=1; if a<=D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per remaining line are lost. The threshold pd is sharp: Z^p-XY^(p-1) refutes any lifting rule based solely on multiplicity-weighted direction count. Under the quoted KTZ premise epsilon>=C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains epsilon/8000.
GLOBAL=The factorwise cleanup, Hasse-height stratification, and sharp weighted-degree separability threshold are proved. They close a characteristic-p hypothesis gap and refute a tempting multiplicity-weighted lifting shortcut, but they do not improve the bivariate benchmark. Under the quoted KTZ hypothesis epsilon >= C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains at least epsilon/8000. The (d/p)^(1-o(1)) target remains open.
STEP [P1] [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: Every irreducible Z-dependent factor has a well-defined p-power Hasse height s; lower positive Hasse derivatives vanish, the order-s derivative is nonzero and coprime, and its weighted degree is at least ds.
PROOF: Write C=sum_k c_k Z^k. All positive supported k are divisible by s, and one has k/s nonzero modulo p. Lucas's theorem gives binom(k,r)=0 for 0<r<s and a nonzero coefficient at r=s. The order-s derivative has smaller Z-degree, hence is coprime to irreducible C. Z-dependence gives weighted degree at least ds.
STEP [P2] [proved] deps=[P1]
STATEMENT: A height-s graph root has vertical multiplicity at least s, and gcd(B,B_Z) is exactly the product of height-greater-than-one factors.
PROOF: Writing C=E(Z^s), a graph root q gives divisibility by Z^s-q^s=(Z-q)^s. Reducing the ordinary product rule for squarefree B modulo each factor shows that the factor divides B_Z exactly when its ordinary Z-derivative vanishes.
STEP [P3] [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: Coprime weighted-degree u,v polynomials of Z-degrees r,s have at most su+rv-drs <= uv/d common affine polynomial graph lines.
PROOF: The nonzero Sylvester resultant has XY-degree at most su+rv-drs. Every common graph line supplies a distinct affine linear factor. The inequality with uv/d follows from (u-dr)(v-ds)>=0. Zero Z-degree cases reduce directly to base-polynomial line factors.
STEP [P4] [proved] deps=[P1];[P3]
STATEMENT: A height-s factor stratum has at most (2r_s-s)Delta_s-d r_s^2 graph lines common with its first nonzero Hasse derivative.
PROOF: The Hasse product rule leaves only one order-s derivative at a time, so the stratum product is coprime to its order-s derivative. That derivative has weighted degree at most Delta_s-ds and Z-degree at most r_s-s. Substitution in [P3] gives the displayed formula.
STEP [P5] [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: Removing at most h content lines reduces every formal A-graph identity to a formal identity for at least one primitive factor of B.
PROOF: From H(L)B(L,q)=0 in the integral domain F_p[t], either H vanishes identically or B(L,q)=0. At most h lines divide H. The factor product identity then forces at least one irreducible factor identity.
STEP [P6] [proved] deps=[P3];[P5]
STATEMENT: The factorwise exceptional-line count is at most sum_i floor(delta_i(delta_i-omega_i)/d)+sum_{i<j} floor(delta_i delta_j/d), hence at most floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d).
PROOF: Apply [P3] to every factor pair and to each factor with its selected derivative. An inseparable irreducible factor has a nonzero X- or Y-derivative because F_p is perfect. Summing and using sum omega_i delta_i=d(Delta-iota)+iota yields the claimed bound.
STEP [P7] [proved] deps=[P6]
STATEMENT: After the exact per-line root deletion, the normalized loss is the theorem's content, exceptional-line, separable-root, and inseparable-root expression, with optional extra loss 1/(p+1).
PROOF: On a unique-factor line, the selected derivative and every other factor restrict to nonzero univariate polynomials. Their degree sum is Delta-omega_i, capped at p. Normalizing line and point deletions in the p^2(p+1)-incidence space gives the stated loss. Degree-at-most-one point pruning costs at most p^2 edges.
STEP [P8] [proved] deps=[P7]
STATEMENT: Every retained point incident to at least two retained graph lines is a simple vertical point of B.
PROOF: At an intersection, uniqueness forces both graph lines onto the same factor. If that factor were inseparable, differentiating along two independent directions would force both base partials to vanish, contradicting retention of the selected derivative. For the resulting separable factor, the product rule makes B_Z nonzero.
STEP [P9] [proved] deps=[P1];[P6];[P7];[P8]
STATEMENT: Weighted degree below pd guarantees vertical separability. For total weighted degree at most D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per other line need be removed.
PROOF: Zero ordinary Z-derivative forces every positive Z-exponent to be a multiple of p, hence weighted degree at least pd. With iota=0, maximizing h+Delta^2/d-Delta subject to h+Delta<=D and Delta>=d yields D^2/d-D; the point budget is D-d.
STEP [P10] [proved] deps=[P9];KTZ Theorem 1.1 and Lemmas 3.2–6.3
STATEMENT: Inserting the proved cleanup into the KTZ parameter envelope leaves the benchmark exponent 1/3 and final agreement epsilon/8000 unchanged.
PROOF: KTZ gives D<=1280(sqrt(dp/epsilon)+1) and D<p. The new exceptional-line term is O(1/(epsilon p)), requiring only epsilon^2 p=Omega(1). Both D/p=O(epsilon) and D<k1/2 require sqrt(dp/epsilon)=O(epsilon p), equivalently epsilon^3=Omega(d/p). The quoted conclusion is agreement at least epsilon/8000.
STEP [P11] [proved] deps=[P1]
STATEMENT: For every campaign pair p,d, a squarefree irreducible polynomial has multiplicity-weighted direction count p(p+1)>pd but no formal graph section.
PROOF: F=Z^p-XY^(p-1) is Eisenstein at X, squarefree, and has weighted degree pd. On direction [a:b], q=ab^(p-1)t gives F(L,Z)=(Z-q)^p. Yet Frobenius p-th powers in F_p[[X,Y]] have all support exponents divisible by p, excluding XY^(p-1).
STEP [P12] [refuted] deps=[P11]

PROOF: For f=xy^(p-1), use X on every line except label the x-axis by zero. Counting gives local agreement (p^2+1)/(p(p+1)); Q=X has global agreement (p(p-1)+1)/p^2.

TITLE=Frobenius Purification and the Singular-Lift Bottleneck
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=For prime p and 101≤d<p: (i) an F_p-irreducible Z-dependent factor of weighted degree δ and Z-degree r with k≥2 absolute factors supports at most (2rδ-dr²)/k²≤δ²/(4d) degree-d graph lines; (ii) for a fixed factor, smooth ramified accepted lifts contribute normalized incidence loss at most 1/(p+1); (iii) if d≤D<2d, a primitive Z-dependent weight-D polynomial is absolutely irreducible and linear in Z, with at most D(D-d) ramified base points; and (iv) for every d≤p-2 there is a minimum-weight D=d+1 primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible interpolant B=XZ+H(Y), whose raw resultant X=0 is a smooth absolutely irreducible plane curve, supporting sp>D fully accepted lines with one ramified point each but no total-degree-d polynomial matching all selected labels. These assertions isolate singular lifted points as the remaining obstruction and do not improve the global soundness exponent.
GLOBAL=A proved architecture-level correction was obtained, but not a stronger soundness exponent. Geometrically reducible factors can be purged by a sharp Frobenius–resultant bound, and smooth ramified lifts cost at most 1/(p+1) for a fixed factor. The unresolved obstruction is singular accepted lifts: an explicit minimum-weight absolutely irreducible interpolant has a smooth absolutely irreducible resultant curve, supports more than D fully accepted graph lines, and still has no global polynomial root matching all labels. Thus absolute irreducibility and plane-curve point counts alone do not improve the KTZ exponent 1/3.
STEP P1 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: Squarefree, Z-primitive B with weighted degree below dp satisfies gcd(B,B_Z)=1.
PROOF: If an irreducible C divides squarefree B and B_Z, then C_Z=0. Since deg_Z(C)<p, C is Z-independent, contradicting primitivity. Z^p-X proves sharpness at weighted degree dp.
STEP P2 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: Two coprime weighted polynomials support at most s u+r v-drs common degree-d graph lines.
PROOF: The coefficient-degree constraints and isobaric monomials in Res_Z(U,V) give degree at most s u+r v-drs. Every common graph line divides the nonzero resultant.
STEP P3 [proved] deps=P2
STATEMENT: An F_p-irreducible, geometrically reducible factor supports at most δ²/(dk²) graph lines.
PROOF: Frobenius acts transitively on the k absolute factors. An F_p graph identity for one factor holds for every conjugate; applying P2 to two conjugates gives (2rδ-dr²)/k²≤δ²/(dk²).
STEP P4 [proved] deps=P3
STATEMENT: All geometrically reducible factors of a primitive weight-D interpolant support at most D²/(4d) graph lines in total, and the constant is sharp.
PROOF: Assign each graph line to an F_p-irreducible factor and use Σδ_i²≤D². The example Z²-νH_0² attains δ²/(4d)=d lines.
STEP P5 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: For one fixed factor, smooth ramified accepted lifts contribute at most p² edges and normalized loss 1/(p+1).
PROOF: At B=B_Z=0, differentiating a formal graph identity gives (B_X,B_Y)·v_L=0. At a smooth lift this nonzero functional has one projective kernel direction, hence at most one incident affine graph line per base point.
STEP P6 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: In the linear-in-Z regime, the ramified base set has at most D(D-d) points and no graph line is identically ramified.
PROOF: D<2d forces B=aZ+b. Primitivity gives gcd(a,b)=1 and absolute irreducibility. Ramified base points lie in V(a,b), so Bézout gives D(D-d); a common ramified graph line would divide both coefficients.
STEP P7 [proved] deps=P1;P6
STATEMENT: The explicit B is primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible and vertically separable, while its raw resultant is a smooth absolutely irreducible line.
PROOF: For B=XZ+H(Y), coefficients X and H are coprime, so Gauss's lemma gives absolute irreducibility. Direct differentiation gives B_Z=X and singular lifts (0,c_j,0); direct resultant computation gives Res_Z(B,B_Z)=X.
STEP P8 [proved] deps=P7
STATEMENT: The explicit uniform-sampling table has selected-line measure s/(p+1) and selected ramified mass s/[p(p+1)].
PROOF: Double roots make H(c_j+mt)/t a degree-at-most-d polynomial. Direct substitution proves the graph identity and agreement with f; counting gives sp selected lines and exactly one B_Z-root on each.
STEP P9 [proved] deps=P8
STATEMENT: No total-degree-d polynomial matches all selected labels, and B is a minimum-weight graph interpolant.
PROOF: If Q matched every selected line, XQ+H would be a degree-D polynomial vanishing on sp>D lines and hence zero, contradicting X∤H. The same argument excludes every nonzero interpolant below weight D.
STEP P10 [proved] deps=P5
STATEMENT: Monicity and smooth absolute irreducibility do not improve the pointwise D-d derivative-root bound.
PROOF: For B=Z²+A(X)Z+Y, B_Y=1 and Δ=A²-4Y has Δ_Y=-4. Both are smooth and absolutely irreducible, while B_Z=A on the graph Y=Z=0 has exactly D-d roots.
STEP P11 [proved] deps=P1
STATEMENT: More than D graph directions and exact per-line ramification saturation can coexist on an absolutely irreducible factor when all bad lifts are singular.
PROOF: For B=Z²-XYg², odd Y-valuation proves absolute irreducibility. Square-slope pencil substitutions give (p-1)/2>D roots, each with d ramified parameters, while the same valuation forbids a polynomial root.
STEP P12 [refuted] deps=P7;P8;P9;P10;P11
STATEMENT: Absolute irreducibility, a smooth resultant curve, or a smooth monic discriminant cannot replace the simple-lift hypothesis by themselves.
PROOF: The explicit constructions respectively contradict the claimed global-root, projected-curve, and per-line conclusions.
STEP P13 [conditional] deps=P3;P5;P12;Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1
STATEMENT: Within the diagnosed deletion architecture, singular ramification remains the term reproducing the cubic exponent.
PROOF: Substitution of D=O(sqrt(dp/ε)) makes purification O(1/(εp)) and generic singular deletion O(sqrt(d/(εp))). The latter is O(ε) only at cubic scale unless singular incidences receive a new bound.
