TITLE=Factorwise Weighted-Polar Accounting and a Sharp Whole-Relation Obstruction
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=For every prime p and integer 101≤d<p, let B∈F_p[X,Y,Z] be primitive in Z, squarefree, of positive Z-degree, and of (1,1,d)-weighted degree δ<p. Factor B=c∏C_i, put δ_i=wdeg(C_i), r_i=deg_Z(C_i), γ_i=wdeg(C_{i,Z}), and Xi_i=(r_i−1)δ_i+r_iγ_i−dr_i(r_i−1). For any disjoint assignment of labeled affine graph lines T=⊔T_i satisfying C_i(L(t),P_L(t))≡0, at most Xi_i lines of T_i are identically C_{i,Z}-ramified, every other assigned graph has total polar intersection multiplicity at most γ_i, and the normalized accepted-incidence cleanup loss is at most sum_i min{|T_i|,Xi_i}/[p(p+1)]+sum_i |T_i|γ_i/[p²(p+1)]. A connected cleaned factor class of minimum degree k>δ_i with 1/sqrt(p+1)≤k/(4p) yields one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with global agreement at least k/(4p). Conversely, for every 2d≤D<p there is an explicit legal table and primitive squarefree relation of weighted degree D for which the whole-relation polar loss is exactly (D−d)/(p+1)=τ(D−d)/p, with every intersection simple; for p≥8d this occurs at ε=Theta((d/p)^(1/3)). The construction has a factor Z with derivative one and a degree-zero global decoder, so it is a proof-architecture obstruction rather than a soundness counterexample.
GLOBAL=A factorwise weighted-resultant and local-intersection-multiplicity lemma is proved. It removes cross-factor collisions from the vertical ramification charge and conditionally yields one global polynomial on any large cleaned factor component. An explicit legal table shows that the unfactored per-line polar bound D−d, including multiplicity accounting, is exactly sharp at the cubic parameter scale. The example is reducible and has a lower-weight factor decoder, so it does not prove sharpness for KTZ's minimum interpolant and does not improve or refute the unconditional 1/3 benchmark.

TITLE=Sharp Incidence-Only Losses in Bivariate Prime-Field Line–Point Agreement
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=Let p be prime, let 101≤d<p, fix m=2, and use uniform affine point-line sampling. There exist f:F_p²→F_p and degree-exactly-d line labels such that ε=d/p, every affine line has exactly d accepted points, every point has accepted degree d or d+1, exactly pd points have degree d+1, and every line supports acceptance. For every θ∈(0,1], the minimum total nonnegative line weight that gives every accepted incidence θ capped coverage through other accepted lines lies between θp(p+1)/(d+1) and θp(p+1)/(d−1); hence every integral such family has size Ω(p/ε). Nevertheless Q=0 has global agreement one. Moreover, for every valid table of agreement ε, the probability Γ₂ that two uniformly chosen distinct lines through a uniform point both accept there satisfies Γ₂=ε²−ε(1−ε)/p+Var_x(deg_acc(x))/[p(p+1)], and the lower bound is attained by accepting exactly k direction classes, ε=k/(p+1).
GLOBAL=No unconditional exponent beyond 1/3 is proved. The new result sharply rules out two incidence-blind improvements: compatible two-line collisions can be only Θ(ε²), and a universal family reaching a constant fraction of every accepted line can require Θ(p/ε) fractional weight even on an exactly almost-regular, fully supported, legal table. Because the extremal tables have the obvious decoder Q=0, the conclusion is a rigorous sparsify-or-decode obstruction, not a counterexample to soundness. KTZ already uses the exact affine Vinh estimate; improving toward exponent 1/2 must attack covering/interpolation or decode the obstructing case, while any exponent above 1/2 also needs a replacement for Vinh-only component recovery in low-d regimes.
STEP P1 [proved] deps=Hall's marriage theorem, proved in the step by regular degree counting
STATEMENT: The affine point-line incidence graph admits a p+1-color decomposition in which every point sees all colors and each line misses one color.
PROOF: Add p dummy point vertices, connecting dummy b to the p finite-slope lines of intercept b and the vertical line x=b. The resulting balanced graph is (p+1)-regular and decomposes iteratively into p+1 perfect matchings. Restriction gives an affine incidence edge-coloring in which every point sees every color and every line misses exactly one.
STEP P2 [proved] deps=P1;Hall's marriage theorem
STATEMENT: There is a spanning affine incidence subgraph with every line of degree d and every point of degree d or d+1.
PROOF: Selecting d colors gives point degree d and line degree d or d−1, with exactly pd deficient lines. In the unselected graph from deficient lines, every left degree is r=p+1−d and every right degree is at most r, so Hall supplies a matching saturating all deficient lines. Adding it gives exact line degree d and point degree d or d+1.
STEP P3 [proved] deps=P2
STATEMENT: The regular incidence subgraph is realized by a legal degree-d line-versus-point table.
PROOF: Set f=0 and, on each fixed line parameterization, take the product of t−a over the d selected incidence parameters. Since d<p, this degree-d polynomial vanishes exactly on the selected points. Counting edges gives ε=d/p, and Q=0 agrees globally everywhere.
STEP P4 [proved] deps=P2;P3
STATEMENT: Every fractional family reaching a θ-fraction of every line through other accepted lines has weight at least θp(p+1)/(d+1).
PROOF: Replace target-excluded loads by the larger common capped load v_x. Double counting gives θd p(p+1)≤Σ_x a_xv_x≤(d+1)Σ_xv_x≤d(d+1)Σ_Lλ_L.
STEP P5 [proved] deps=P2;P3
STATEMENT: The other-line fractional-cover lower bound is tight within factor (d+1)/(d−1).
PROOF: Assign every line weight θ/(d−1). At every accepted incidence, at least d−1 other accepted lines contribute, so its capped other-line load is at least θ. Total weight is θp(p+1)/(d−1).
STEP P6 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: The ordered distinct-line joint-acceptance probability equals ε²−ε(1−ε)/p+Var(a_x)/[p(p+1)].
PROOF: At point x there are exactly a_x(a_x−1) ordered pairs of distinct accepted incident lines. Averaging and expanding E[a_x²] as E[a_x]²+Var(a_x), with E[a_x]=(p+1)ε, gives the exact formula.
STEP P7 [proved] deps=P6
STATEMENT: The collision lower bound is attained by concentrated good directions for every ε=k/(p+1).
PROOF: Accept all incidences on k direction classes using zero labels and reject all others using constant-one labels, with f=0. Every point has accepted degree k, so the variance vanishes and Γ₂=k(k−1)/[p(p+1)].
STEP P8 [proved] deps=Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Revision 1, Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1–4.3
STATEMENT: The KTZ cubic threshold is reconstructed from covering, interpolation, and root supply.
PROOF: The source gives |R|=O(p/ε), weighted degree D=O(sqrt(d|R|)), and requires D=O(εp). Squaring yields ε³=Ω(d/p). The probabilistic selector losses are constants and KTZ does not claim simultaneous Θ-saturation.
STEP P9 [proved] deps=Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Revision 1, Lemmas 2.1 and 6.2
STATEMENT: Vinh-only component recovery has a method barrier at exponent 1/2 in the legal lower-boundary regime.
PROOF: The component argument requires (p+1)^−1/2≤ε/8000. For d=101 and ε=C(101/p)^α with fixed α>1/2, εsqrt(p) tends to zero, so this Vinh-based recovery step cannot yield linear component size.
STEP P10 [proved] deps=Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorems 3 and 4
STATEMENT: The quoted stronger prime-field incidence bounds do not supply linear component recovery within their own characteristic ranges.
PROOF: For balanced supports, kN≪N^(22/15) yields N≫k^(15/7); making this as large as kp forces parameters outside N≪p^(15/11). In the balanced Cartesian case, kN≪N^(11/8)+N yields N≫k^(8/3); making this as large as kp forces parameters outside N≪p^(4/3).
STEP P11 [refuted] deps=P4;P5;P6;P7
STATEMENT: Acceptance density alone always yields either more than quadratic collision energy or an o(p/ε)-weight universal reaching family.
PROOF: The regular carrier rules out a universally smaller reaching family, and the direction table attains the collision square. Both have Q=0, so they refute only the incidence-only shortcut, not soundness.

Vinh's main term is exact on these projections, so unweighted incidence bounds discard the acceptance information.","Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 3 requires comparable distinct sets and a characteristic-size condition; the component-size exponent it supplies becomes linear only outside that range.","Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 4 additionally requires a Cartesian point set and likewise has no parameter overlap where its consequence supplies linear recovery.","For fixed legal d and a candidate soundness exponent above 1/2, the KTZ/Vinh mixing requirement ε=Ω(p^−1/2) fails.","The extremal incidence examples are easy to decode, so none refutes the soundness target; they force a dichotomy rather than establish impossibility."]

TITLE=Sharp Higher-Moment Envelopes and Raw-DRC Barriers for Good Affine Incidences
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=Fix a prime p, an integer 101<=d<p, and m=2. For every valid table of local agreement epsilon, if a_x and b_L are accepted point and line degrees and J_s is the adjacent-integer interpolation of k↦(k)_s, then Gamma_s^P>=J_s((p+1)epsilon)/(p+1)_s for 0<=s<=p+1 and Gamma_s^L>=J_s(p epsilon)/(p)_s for 0<=s<=p; with-replacement moments are at least epsilon^r. There is an explicit connected legal table at epsilon=d/p attaining both distinct-star envelopes simultaneously for every admissible s, with every line degree d and point degrees d or d+1. For every 0<theta<=1 its fractional other-line reaching optimum lies between theta p(p+1)/(d+1) and theta p(p+1)/(d-1), while Q=0 agrees globally everywhere. Every raw accepted graph is K_{2,2}-free. For the auxiliary line-compatibility graph H, an exact t-th degree-moment DRC bound and a concurrency correction yield at least M_t(H)-binom(t,2)(p+1)/(t-h+1) common-neighbor target lines with h distinct seed intersections.
GLOBAL=A fully quantified all-order moment and dependent-random-choice lemma is proved. Degree-based star moments are sharply minimized by a new explicit connected legal carrier at epsilon=d/p, while raw affine incidence DRC collapses because the graph is K_{2,2}-free and fractional universal reaching still costs Theta(theta p/epsilon). An exact auxiliary compatibility-graph DRC certificate is also proved, but its acceptance-density-only specialization raises epsilon^2 to a t-th power and is quantitatively useless for the t>256d root supply required by the direct KTZ interpolation route. These are proof-architecture obstructions and tools, not a soundness counterexample: the carrier has the perfect decoder Q=0. The unconditional benchmark exponent 1/3 is not improved.
STEP P1 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: For every valid table, Gamma_s^P=E_x[(a_x)_s]/(p+1)_s and Gamma_s^L=E_L[(b_L)_s]/(p)_s in their stated ranges.
PROOF: Double counting gives E_x a_x=(p+1)epsilon and E_L b_L=p epsilon. Conditional on the center, the number of ordered distinct accepted tuples is the appropriate falling factorial.
STEP P2 [proved] deps=P1
STATEMENT: For every valid table, the point and line distinct-star probabilities satisfy the J_s lower bounds, and both with-replacement probabilities are at least epsilon^r.
PROOF: For F_s(j)=(j)_s, the second forward difference is s(s-1)(j)_(s-2)>=0. Jensen applied to its piecewise-linear interpolation gives the adjacent-integer envelope. Ordinary Jensen gives the with-replacement epsilon^r bounds.
STEP P3 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: For every prime p and 101<=d<p, the explicit table has epsilon=d/p, every line degree d, exactly pd point vertices of degree d+1, and every other point degree d.
PROOF: Take S_d={0,...,d-1}, f=0, P_{m,b}(t)=product_{s in S_d}(t+m-s) on L_{m,b}(t)=(t,mt+b), and P_{V_c}(t)=product_{s in S_d}(t-s) on vertical lines. Root counting gives line degree d; slope counting gives point degree d or d+1.
STEP P4 [proved] deps=P3
STATEMENT: The accepted graph of the explicit table is connected and uses every point and affine line.
PROOF: Using 0,1,2 in S_d gives a four-edge path from (x,y) to (x,y+1) through (x-1,y+x-1), and an accepted edge-path from (x,y) to (x+1,y-x). These moves connect all point vertices; every line has a neighbor.
STEP P5 [proved] deps=P2;P3
STATEMENT: The explicit table simultaneously attains both distinct-star moment lower envelopes for every admissible order and attains the repeated line moment epsilon^r for every r>=1.
PROOF: The point-degree law is precisely the adjacent mixture at mean d+d/p, while the line degree is constantly d. Substitution gives equality in every moment envelope; constant line degree also gives repeated line moment (d/p)^r.
STEP P6 [proved] deps=P3
STATEMENT: For every 0<theta<=1 on the explicit table, theta p(p+1)/(d+1)<=OPT_theta<=theta p(p+1)/(d-1).
PROOF: Capped-load double counting gives theta*d*p(p+1)<=d(d+1)sum_M lambda_M. Uniform weights theta/(d-1) give the matching upper bound.
STEP P7 [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: Every accepted affine incidence graph is K_{2,2}-free, and two or more distinct vertices on either shore have at most one common neighbor.
PROOF: Two distinct affine points determine one line and two distinct affine lines meet in at most one point.
STEP P8 [proved] deps=P1;P2
STATEMENT: For every valid table, e(H)=sum_x binom(a_x,2) and average_degree(H)>=p*mu(mu-1)/(p+1), where mu=(p+1)epsilon.
PROOF: Each auxiliary edge is a unique pair of accepted lines through its intersection. Summing binom(a_x,2) and applying convexity gives the displayed average-degree bound.
STEP P9 [proved] deps=P8
STATEMENT: For every 1<=t<=floor(average_degree(H)), some distinct t-line seed set has at least M_t(H)>=N binom(floor(average_degree(H)),t)/binom(N,t) common neighbors; under the extra stated hypotheses this is at least N(epsilon^2/4)^t.
PROOF: Double count pairs consisting of a t-seed set contained in a vertex neighborhood. Discrete convexity of binom(j,t) yields the average-degree specialization; elementary product bounds give N(epsilon^2/4)^t.
STEP P10 [proved] deps=P9
STATEMENT: For every 1<=h<=t, the seed set in P9 has at least M_t(H)-binom(t,2)(p+1)/(t-h+1) common neighbors with at least h distinct seed-intersection points.
PROOF: A target with fewer than h distinct seed intersections creates at least t-h+1 colliding seed pairs. Each seed pair can collide on at most p+1 target lines. Double counting gives the subtraction term.
STEP P11 [proved] deps=P10;KTZ Lemmas 2.2 and 4.1--4.3
STATEMENT: For fixed admissible d and t>256d, the acceptance-density-only auxiliary DRC certificate is asymptotically vacuous at the cubic scale as p tends to infinity.
PROOF: KTZ's degree D_t=16(floor(sqrt(dt))+1) and h<=t make h>D_t imply t>256d. At epsilon=C(d/p)^(1/3) with fixed d,t, the density-only term is O(p^(2-2t/3)), hence tends to zero.
STEP P12 [proved] deps=P3;P4;P5;P6;P7
STATEMENT: Connectivity, full support, degree-moment extremality, and Theta(p/epsilon) universal reaching cost do not individually or jointly obstruct a single perfect global decoder.
PROOF: The same connected table has full support, sharp minimal moment profiles, and sharp universal reaching cost, while Q=0 agrees at every point.
STEP P13 [proved] deps=KTZ Theorem 1.1;KTZ Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1--4.3
STATEMENT: The KTZ selector/interpolation/transfer chain reconstructs the benchmark exponent 1/3 and global agreement Omega(epsilon).
PROOF: Substitute |R|=O(p/epsilon) into D=O(sqrt(d|R|)), then impose D=O(epsilon p). Squaring and rearranging gives epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).

TITLE=Popularity-Sensitive Core Decoding and a Bounded-Star-Energy Obstruction
STATUS=proved
THEOREM=For C_*=2^60, any nonempty accepted subgraph with point minimum degree u, line minimum degree v, line measure tau, and w=min(u,v), satisfying uv>=C_*dp and uw^2>=C_*d tau p^2, yields one total-degree-at-most-d Q with agreement at least v/(800p). Consequently, if the accepted-edge upper-tail threshold u_* satisfies u_*epsilon^2>=2^70d, then one Q has agreement at least epsilon/12800. The implication is proved, but its improved exponent requires an additional popularity hypothesis not known universally.
GLOBAL=A proved asymmetric core-decoding lemma isolates point popularity as a useful resource: u_*epsilon^2 >= 2^70 d suffices for linear global agreement. Under u_* >= kappa epsilon^a p this gives the conditional exponent 1/(a+2), reaching 1/2 but not beyond. The universal popularity bound recovers only exponent 1/3, so the benchmark is not improved. A diffuse modal construction additionally proves that no scale-free bounded-order star-energy increment can supply the missing popularity; a successful refinement must be algebra-aware and restricted to the target range.
STEP [P1] [proved] deps=KTZ Lemma 2.1
STATEMENT: Asymmetric mixing gives tau>=u/[4(p+1)], |X|/p^2>=v/(4p), and w^2>=2^57dp.
PROOF: Normalize point measure xi, line measure tau, and edge measure rho. Minimum degrees give rho>=u xi/(p+1) and rho>=v tau/p, while affine mixing gives rho<=xi tau+sqrt(xi tau/(p+1)). Assuming either tau<u/[4(p+1)] or xi<v/(4p) forces uv<2p. The theorem hypotheses contradict this. Substitution into the second gate yields w^2>=2^57dp.
STEP [P2] [proved] deps=[P1];Chernoff bounds
STATEMENT: There is a selector R of size Theta(t/u) reaching more than half of every target line's accepted points.
PROOF: Sample each line at rate 8/u and define reach through R minus the target line. Along a fixed target line, the relevant random-line sets are disjoint, so reach indicators are independent. Failure is at most exp(-v/24). The bound w^2>=2^57dp makes the union over fewer than 2p^2 lines valid. Sample-size Chernoff bounds give 4t/u<=|R|<=16t/u.
STEP [P3] [proved] deps=[P2];KTZ Lemmas 2.2, 2.3, and 2.5
STATEMENT: A squarefree Z-dependent explainer A of weighted degree at most D exists, with D<min(u,v)/5000 and A_Z nonzero.
PROOF: For D=16(floor(sqrt(d|R|))+1), one has D^2<=2^-45w^2, D<p, and |R|>=p>D. The characteristic-compatible monomial space has dimension exceeding |R|(D+1). A nonzero Z-dependent candidate exists; minimizing weighted degree among pointwise interpolants with nonzero Z-derivative gives a squarefree A. Since D<p, all selected pointwise identities are formal.
STEP [P4] [proved] deps=[P2];[P3]
STATEMENT: The explainer identity extends from selected lines to every line in the accepted core.
PROOF: Every target line has more than v/2>D accepted points reached by another selected line. At each such point the two line values equal f. Thus the degree-at-most-D restriction of A has more than D roots and vanishes formally.
STEP [P5] [proved] deps=[P4];KTZ Lemma 2.6
STATEMENT: Prime-field cleanup produces a Z-primitive relation with simple retained roots after deleting at most pD^2+Dt edges.
PROOF: Remove the Z-content H from A. The squarefree Z-primitive factor B has gcd(B,B_Z)=1 because deg_Z B<p. There are at most D content lines, D(D-1) resultant-degenerate lines, and D derivative-zero accepted points on every remaining line, totaling at most pD^2+Dt exceptional edges.
STEP [P6] [proved] deps=[P1];[P3];[P5]
STATEMENT: Asymmetric pruning preserves a nonempty simple-root graph with minima at least floor(u/100) and floor(v/100).
PROOF: The exceptional fraction is at most D/v+2^14dp/(uv)<1/4000. Peeling below floor(u/100) on points and floor(v/100) on lines costs at most |E|/50, so a nonempty graph remains with both minima above D.
STEP [P7] [proved] deps=[P1];[P6];KTZ Lemmas 6.1 and 6.3
STATEMENT: One total-degree-at-most-d polynomial has global agreement at least v/(800p).
PROOF: At any surviving point, more than D incident line roots and B_Z nonzero invoke KTZ lifting. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same Q through a component. Asymmetric mixing gives that component point density at least floor(v/100)/(4p)>=v/(800p).
STEP [P8] [conditional] deps=[P7]
STATEMENT: A popularity tail u_*epsilon^2>=2^70d suffices; u_*>=kappa epsilon^a p gives the conditional exponent 1/(a+2).
PROOF: The u_* tail has normalized mass at least epsilon/2. Peeling at U=floor(u_*/2) and V=floor(delta_*p/4) leaves a core with U>=u_*/3, V>=epsilon p/16. The condition u_*epsilon^2>=2^70d verifies both gates of Theorem A and yields agreement epsilon/12800. The further hypothesis u_*>=kappa epsilon^a p translates this to exponent 1/(a+2).
STEP [P9] [proved] deps=KTZ Lemma 2.1;Cauchy-Schwarz
STATEMENT: At most delta(p+1) algebraic templates carrying mass delta force one template with global agreement at least delta/4.
PROOF: Apply affine mixing to each template's agreement set and line family. Sum the main terms and use Cauchy-Schwarz on the errors to obtain delta<=alpha tau+sqrt(R alpha tau/(p+1)). If R<=delta(p+1), alpha<delta/4 makes the right side less than delta.
STEP [P10] [proved] deps=[P9]
STATEMENT: Sufficient accepted mass on constant-labelled lines directly yields one popular constant global polynomial.
PROOF: For constant labels, the corresponding point agreement sets are disjoint, so the sum of their measures is one and the template-count loss disappears. This yields delta<=mu tau+sqrt(tau/(p+1)) and the stated decoding branch.
STEP [P11] [proved] deps=
STATEMENT: All deterministic tables satisfy an exact falling-factorial star identity and its sharp degree-only lower bound.
PROOF: Condition on a point of accepted degree a_x and sample ordered distinct incident lines. This gives Gamma_s=E[(a_x)_s]/(p+1)_s. Discrete convexity yields the sharp interpolation between adjacent integer degrees; expanding s=2 gives the exact variance identity.
STEP [P12] [proved] deps=Second-moment inequality;McDiarmid inequality
STATEMENT: The modal construction has local agreement Theta(log p/(p log log p)) with explicit constants.
PROOF: For iid point values and independent symmetric modal ties, occupancy estimates give r/(65p)<=q<=17r/p. A one-point change alters epsilon by at most 1/p^2, so McDiarmid places realized epsilon in [q/2,3q/2] with failure at most 1/4.
STEP [P13] [proved] deps=[P12];Chernoff bound;Markov inequality
STATEMENT: A deterministic modal table simultaneously has baseline bounded-order energy and global degree-101 agreement below 2/p.
PROOF: Conditioned on f(x), the punctured incident lines and symmetric tie coins are independent, giving E[Gamma_s]=q^s. Markov plus a union over fixed s costs 1/4. A union bound over p^5253 degree-101 polynomials costs at most p^5253(e/4)^p, and the line-maximum event costs at most 2p^3/v_0!. The remaining probability is positive.
STEP [P14] [refuted] deps=[P13]
STATEMENT: A scale-free claim that lack of a decoder forces Gamma_s>=c epsilon^(s-gamma) is false.
PROOF: In the modal tables, global agreement divided by epsilon tends to zero, but Gamma_s/epsilon^(s-gamma)=O_s(epsilon^gamma) also tends to zero. Hence the proposed implication fails for every fixed s, gamma, and positive constants.

	researcher-0007	P5				Let $p$ be prime and let $101\\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\\delta<p$, with\n\n$$\nB=c\\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i.\n$$\n\nFor each $i$, put\n\n$$\n\\delta_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),\n\\quad\nr_i=\\deg_Z(C_i),\n\\quad\n\\gamma_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),\n$$\n\nand\n\n$$\n\\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\\delta_i+r_i\\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).\n$$\n\nLet $\\mathcal T=\\bigsqcup_i\\mathcal T_i$ be a disjoint assignment of distinct affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$, satisfying\n\n$$\nC_i(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0\n\\qquad(L\\in\\mathcal T_i).\n$$\n\nFor each $i$, let\n\n$$\ne_i=\n\\left|\n\\left\\{L\\in\\mathcal T_i:\nC_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0\n\\right\\}\n\\right|.\n$$\n\nFor a point table $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$, let $i(L)=i$ when $L\\in\\mathcal T_i$, and define\n\n$$\n\\mathcal R=\n\\left\\{(L,t):\n\\begin{array}{l}\nL\\in\\mathcal T,\\ t\\in\\mathbb F_p,\\\\\nP_L(t)=f(L(t)),\\\\\nC_{i(L),Z}(L(t),f(L(t)))=0\n\\end{array}\n\\right\\}.\n$$\n\nThen\n\n$$\ne_i\\le\\min\\{|\\mathcal T_i|,\\Xi_i\\},\n$$\n\n$$\n|\\mathcal R|\n\\le\n\\sum_i\\left(pe_i+(|\\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\\gamma_i\\right),\n$$\n\nand\n\n$$\n\\frac{|\\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}\n\\le\n\\sum_i\\frac{\\min\\{|\\mathcal T_i|,\\Xi_i\\}}{p(p+1)}\n+\n\\sum_i\\frac{|\\mathcal T_i|\\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.\n$$\n\nMoreover,\n\n$$\n\\gamma_i\\le\\delta_i-d\n$$\n\nand\n\n$$\n\\Xi_i\\le(2r_i-1)\\delta_i-dr_i^2\n\\le\\frac{\\delta_i^2}{d}-\\delta_i.\n$$\n\nIf\n\n$$\n\\tau_i=\\frac{|\\mathcal T_i|}{p(p+1)},\n\\qquad\n\\tau=\\sum_i\\tau_i>0,\n\\qquad\n\\bar\\delta=\\frac{\\sum_i\\tau_i\\delta_i}{\\tau},\n$$\n\nthen\n\n$$\n\\frac{|\\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}\n\\le\n\\frac{\\delta^2/d-\\delta}{p(p+1)}\n+\n\\frac{\\tau(\\bar\\delta-d)}p.\n$$
	researcher-0007	P6				Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P5.1, and fix $i$. Let $G=(\\mathcal X,T',E')$ be a connected bipartite graph with $\\mathcal X\\subseteq\\mathbb F_p^2$, $T'\\subseteq\\mathcal T_i$, and\n\n$$\nE'\\subseteq\n\\{(x,L)\\in\\mathcal X\\times T':x\\in L,\\ P_L(x)=f(x)\\}.\n$$\n\nSuppose $G$ has minimum degree at least the integer $k$, and suppose\n\n$$\nC_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\\ne0\n\\qquad((x,L)\\in E').\n$$\n\nIf\n\n$$\nk>\\delta_i\n\\qquad\\text{and}\\qquad\n\\frac1{\\sqrt{p+1}}\\le\\frac{k}{4p},\n$$\n\nthen there exists $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that\n\n$$\nQ(x)=f(x)\n\\qquad(x\\in\\mathcal X)\n$$\n\nand\n\n$$\n\\frac{|\\{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x)=f(x)\\}|}{p^2}\n\\ge\\frac{k}{4p}.\n$$
	researcher-0007	P7				Let $p$ be prime, and let $d,D$ be integers satisfying\n\n$$\n101\\le d,\n\\qquad\n2d\\le D<p.\n$$\n\nPut $e=D-d$, choose $S\\subseteq\\mathbb F_p$ with $|S|=e$, and define\n\n$$\nH(X)=\\prod_{a\\in S}(X-a),\n\\qquad\nB=Z(Z-H(X)).\n$$\n\nDefine $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$ by\n\n$$\nf(x,y)=\n\\begin{cases}\n0,&x\\in S,\\\\\n1,&x\\notin S,\n\\end{cases}\n$$\n\nand assign the constant label $P_L=0$ to every nonvertical affine line and the constant label $P_L=2$ to every vertical affine line. 	researcher-0007	P8				Let $p,d,D,e,S,H,B,f$ and the line labels be as in researcher-0007:P7.1, and put\n\n$$\n\\tau=\\frac{p}{p+1}.\n$$\n\nOn every nonvertical zero-labelled graph, $B_Z(L(t),0)$ has exactly $D-d$ simple roots, and these roots are exactly the accepted parameters. 	researcher-0007	P9				Let $p$ be prime and let $d$ be an integer satisfying\n\n$$\n101\\le d\n\\qquad\\text{and}\\qquad\np\\ge8d.\n$$\n\nSet\n\n$$\ne=\\left\\lceil(dp^2)^{1/3}\\right\\rceil,\n\\qquad\nD=d+e,\n\\qquad\n\\varepsilon=\\frac e{p+1}.\n$$\n\nThen\n\n$$\ne\\ge4d,\n\\qquad\ne\\le\\frac p2+1,\n\\qquad\n2d\\le D<p,\n$$\n\nand\n\n$$\n\\frac12\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1/3}\n\\le\\varepsilon\n\\le2\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1/3}.\n$$\n\nFor the construction of researcher-0007:P7.1 and researcher-0007:P8.1,\n\n$$\nD=\\Theta(\\varepsilon p)\n\\qquad\\text{and}\\qquad\nD=\\Theta\\!\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{dp}{\\varepsilon}}\\right).\n$$

	researcher-0008	P1				Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, let $\\mathcal P=\\mathbb F_p^2$, let $\\mathcal L$ be the set of affine lines in $\\mathbb F_p^2$, and let\n\n$$E=\\{(x,L)\\in\\mathcal P\\times\\mathcal L:x\\in L\\}.$$\n\nThere is a partition of $E$ into $p+1$ color classes such that every point is incident to exactly one edge of each color, every affine line is incident to exactly one edge of all but one color and to no edge of the remaining color, and every color is omitted by exactly $p$ affine lines.
	researcher-0008	P2				Let $p$ be prime and let $101\\le d<p$. The affine point-line incidence graph over $\\mathbb F_p^2$ has a spanning subgraph in which every affine line has degree $d$, exactly $pd$ points have degree $d+1$, and the remaining $p(p-d)$ points have degree $d$.
	researcher-0008	P3				Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, and let $H$ be the spanning incidence subgraph supplied by P2. 	researcher-0008	P4				For the table constructed in P3, write $\\mathcal L$ for the affine lines, $A(x,L)\\in\\{0,1\\}$ for the acceptance indicator,\n\n$$N_E(L)=\\{x\\in L:A(x,L)=1\\},\\qquad a_x=\\sum_{L\\ni x}A(x,L).$$\n\nLet $0<\\theta\\le1$, let $\\lambda=(\\lambda_M)_{M\\in\\mathcal L}$ be nonnegative line weights, and define\n\n$$u_{x,L}(\\lambda)=\\min\\left\\{1,\\sum_{\\substack{M\\ni x\\\\M\\ne L}}A(x,M)\\lambda_M\\right\\}$$\n\nfor every accepted incidence $(x,L)$. 	researcher-0008	P5				For the table constructed in P3 and every $0<\\theta\\le1$, assign every affine line the weight\n\n$$\\lambda_M=\\frac{\\theta}{d-1}.$$\n\nThese weights are $\\theta$-reaching through other accepted lines and have total weight\n\n$$\\frac{\\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$\n\nIf $\\operatorname{OPT}_\\theta$ is the infimum of the total weight over all nonnegative $\\theta$-reaching weights, then\n\n$$\\operatorname{OPT}_\\theta\\le\\frac{\\theta p(p+1)}{d-1},$$\n\nand the multiplicative gap between this upper bound and the lower-bound value $\\theta p(p+1)/(d+1)$ is\n\n$$\\frac{d+1}{d-1}\\le\\frac{102}{100}.$$
	researcher-0008	P6				Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, and consider any valid affine line-versus-point table over $\\mathbb F_p^2$ with line-label degree at most $d$. 	researcher-0009	P1				Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\\mathbb F_p^2$ have agreement $\\epsilon$, accepted point degrees $a_x$, and accepted line degrees $b_L$. 	researcher-0010	[P2]				Under the hypotheses of the asymmetric mixing bounds, there exists $R\\subseteq T$ such that\n\n$$\n\\frac{4t}{u}\\le |R|\\le\\frac{16t}{u}\n\\quad\\text{and}\\quad\n|R|\\ge p,\n$$\n\nand, for every $L\\in T$, more than half of the points $x\\in N_G(L)$ are incident to a line $M\\in R\\setminus\\{L\\}$ with $(x,M)\\in E$.
	researcher-0010	[P3]				Let $R$ be a selector from the preceding entry, put $r=|R|$, and define\n\n$$\nD=16\\bigl(\\lfloor\\sqrt{dr}\\rfloor+1\\bigr).\n$$\n\nThere exists a nonzero squarefree, $Z$-dependent polynomial $A\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ such that\n\n$$\nA_Z\\not\\equiv0,\n\\qquad\nA(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0\n$$\n\nfor every $L\\in R$, and\n\n$$\nD<\\frac{\\min\\{u,v\\}}{5000}<p.\n$$

STATEMENT=Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Assign weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $C=\sum_{k\ge0}c_k(X,Y)Z^k\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent, and define

$$e:=\min\{v_p(k):k>0,\ c_k\ne0\},\qquad s:=p^e.$$

For the Hasse derivatives determined by

$$C(X,Y,Z+T)=\sum_{r\ge0}\partial_Z^{[r]}C(X,Y,Z)T^r,$$

one has $\partial_Z^{[r]}C=0$ for every $0<r<s$, while $\partial_Z^{[s]}C\ne0$, $\gcd(C,\partial_Z^{[s]}C)=1$, and $\operatorname{wdeg}C\ge ds$.
STATUS=proved

SOURCE researcher-0005:[P2]
ID=researcher-0005:[P2].1
STATEMENT=Let $B=c\prod_{i=1}^r C_i\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$, where $c\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ and the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent polynomials with Hasse heights $s_i$. If an affine-line parametrization $L(t)$ and a polynomial $q(t)\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfy

in $\mathbb F_p[t,Z]$. Moreover, $\gcd(B,B_Z)$ has exactly the irreducible factors $C_i$ for which $s_i>1$.

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\sum_i\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i(\delta_i-\omega_i)}{d}\right\rfloor
+
\sum_{i<j}\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i\delta_j}{d}\right\rfloor$$

and

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^2-d\Delta+(d-1)\iota}{d}\right\rfloor.$$

Let $C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}$ be the absolute constant in the quoted KTZ theorem. If

$$\epsilon\ge C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3},$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$\frac{|\{(x,y)\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x,y)=f(x,y)\}|}{p^2}\ge\frac{\epsilon}{8000}.$$
STATUS=proved

STATEMENT=Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Define

$$F(X,Y,Z):=Z^p-XY^{p-1}.$$

Then $F$ is irreducible and squarefree, $\operatorname{wdeg}F=pd$, $F_Z=0$, $\partial_Z^{[r]}F=0$ for $1\le r<p$, and $\partial_Z^{[p]}F=1$. For every $[a:b]\in\mathbb P^1(\mathbb F_p)$, set

$$L_{a,b}(t):=(at,bt),
\qquad
q_{a,b}(t):=ab^{p-1}t.$$

Then

$$F(L_{a,b}(t),Z)=(Z-q_{a,b}(t))^p.$$

Consequently, the multiplicity-weighted direction count is

$$p(p+1)>pd=\operatorname{wdeg}F,$$

but there is no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[[X,Y]]$ satisfying $F(X,Y,Q(X,Y))=0$.

SOURCE researcher-0007:P5
ID=researcher-0007:P5.1
STATEMENT=Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, with

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i.
$$

For each $i$, put

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\quad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
\quad
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T=\bigsqcup_i\mathcal T_i$ be a disjoint assignment of distinct affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$, satisfying

$$
C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\qquad(L\in\mathcal T_i).
$$

For each $i$, let

$$
e_i=
\left|
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
\right|.
$$

For a point table $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, let $i(L)=i$ when $L\in\mathcal T_i$, and define

$$
\mathcal R=
\left\{(L,t):
\begin{array}{l}
L\in\mathcal T,\ t\in\mathbb F_p,\\
P_L(t)=f(L(t)),\\
C_{i(L),Z}(L(t),f(L(t)))=0
\end{array}
\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
e_i\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\},
$$

$$
|\mathcal R|
\le
\sum_i\left(pe_i+(|\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\gamma_i\right),
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\sum_i\frac{\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}}{p(p+1)}
+
\sum_i\frac{|\mathcal T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.
$$

Moreover,

$$
\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i\le(2r_i-1)\delta_i-dr_i^2
\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.
$$

If

$$
\tau_i=\frac{|\mathcal T_i|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\tau=\sum_i\tau_i>0,
\qquad
\bar\delta=\frac{\sum_i\tau_i\delta_i}{\tau},
$$

then

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\frac{\delta^2/d-\delta}{p(p+1)}
+
\frac{\tau(\bar\delta-d)}p.
$$
STATUS=proved
